# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-04-25_TotalExtraction_84717341.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION ADOPTING A CITY OF CHEYENNE HEALTHCARE 
PLAN, A CITY OF CHEYENNE HEALTH BENEFIT TRUST, AND APPOINTING 
FIDUCIARIES FOR THE PLAN AND TRUST” 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne wishes to create a self-insured 
employee welfare benefit plan to be named the City of Cheyenne Healthcare Plan (“the Plan”) to 
provide medical, dental, vision, and prescription drug benefits (“Plan Benefits”) for eligible 
employees of the City of Cheyenne (“the Employer”) and eligible dependents of the eligible 
employees;  
 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne wishes to adopt a City of 
Cheyenne Health Benefit Trust (“the Trust”) to constitute a trust fund to hold employer, 
employee, and other contributions to be used to provide Plan Benefits; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne wishes to appoint fiduciaries 
for the Plan and the Trust, including Trustees, the Plan Sponsor, and the Plan Administrator.   
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that: 
 
 
The City of Cheyenne does hereby adopt the Trust Agreement for the City of Cheyenne 
Health Benefit Trust in the form presented to the Governing Body, a copy of which is attached 
hereto, for the purpose of establishing a trust for the provision of employee welfare benefits 
pursuant to the City of Cheyenne Healthcare Plan; 
 
 
The Trustees of the Trust shall be the City Treasurer, the City Attorney, and the City 
Council President and Robin Lockman, currently serving as City Treasurer, Stefanie Boster, 
currently serving as City Attorney, and Scott Roybal, currently serving as City Council 
President, are appointed to serve as the initial trustees under the Trust; 
 
 
The Employee Benefits Committee of the City of Cheyenne is appointed to discharge the 
responsibilities of the Plan Sponsor for the City of Cheyenne Healthcare Plan; 
 
 
The Human Resources Director of the City of Cheyenne, Darrin Hass, is appointed as the 
Plan Administrator for the City of Cheyenne Healthcare Plan and he may delegate some of his 
responsibilities to the City’s Human Resources Benefit Specialist, and to Third Party 
Administrators hired to administer the Plan;   
 
 
The City Mayor and the Trustees are hereby authorized to execute and deliver, in the 
name and on behalf of the City of Cheyenne, the Trust Agreement for the City of Cheyenne 
Health Benefit Trust; and 
 
 
The City Mayor and the Plan Administrator are hereby authorized to execute contracts 
with Third Party Administrators for the City of Cheyenne Healthcare Plan and all such other 
By Stefanie Boster at 1:53 pm, Mar 04, 2022

instruments and documents, and to do and perform all such acts as shall be necessary and proper 
to establish and administer the Plan and otherwise effect the purposes and intentions set forth in 
the foregoing resolutions. 
 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this ______ day of ________________, 2022. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
__________________________________________ 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Patrick J. Collins, Mayor 
(SEAL) 
Attest: 
 
__________________________________ 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 

CITY OF CHEYENNE  
HEALTH BENEFIT TRUST AGREEMENT  
 
 
 
THIS TRUST AGREEMENT is made and entered into as of ___________, 2022, by and 
between the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming (the “Employer”) and the persons signing this 
Agreement as Trustees (the “Trustees”).  The name of this Trust is the City of Cheyenne Health 
Benefit Trust.  
 
 
WHEREAS, the Employer has established and adopted an employee welfare benefit plan 
known as the City of Cheyenne Healthcare Plan (the “Plan”) the purpose of which is to provide 
medical, dental, vision, and prescription drug benefits (“Plan Benefits”) for the Employer’s 
eligible employees and their eligible dependents; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Employer has appointed a Plan Administrator to administer the Plan and 
will hire Third Party Administrators to provide administrative services and claims processing for 
Plan Benefits offered by the Plan; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the funds that will be contributed to this Trust, as and when received by the 
Trustees, will constitute a trust fund (the "Trust Fund") to be held for the benefit of Employer’s 
participating eligible employees and their participating eligible dependents under and in 
accordance with the Plan;  
 
 
WHEREAS, the Employer intends that this Trust shall be governed by the Wyoming 
Uniform Trust Code, Wyo. Stat. §§ 4-10-101 et seq., shall be tax-exempt pursuant to 26 U.S.C. § 
115, and, when taken together with the Plan, shall constitute a “welfare benefit fund” pursuant to 
26 U.S.C. § 419(e)(1); 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Employer desires the Trustees to hold and administer the Trust Fund, 
and the Trustees are willing to hold and administer such Trust Fund, pursuant to the terms of this 
Trust Agreement.  
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the mutual covenants contained herein, the 
Employer and the Trustees agree as follows:  
 
 
ARTICLE I – DEFINED TERMS 
 
 
The terms used in this Trust Agreement are the same as used in the Plan Document and 
Summary Plan Description for the City of Cheyenne Healthcare Plan, unless otherwise stated.  A 
Plan Participant is an Employer’s eligible employee or an employee's eligible dependent who is 
covered under the Plan.   
 
 
 
 
By Stefanie Boster at 1:47 pm, Mar 04, 2022

 
 
Page 2 of  9 
ARTICLE II – CONTRIBUTIONS  
 
 
Receipt of Contributions. The Trustees shall receive the contributions paid to them by the 
Employer or a Plan Participant.  The amount of such contributions shall be determined by the 
Employer.  Such contributions may include required monthly contributions as determined by the 
Employer from time to time to fund the Plan Benefits offered by the Employer and elected by 
Plan Participants and the payment of Plan expenses.  Contributions also include stop loss 
insurance reimbursements and drug prescription rebates received by the Trustees.  Contributions 
may also include any additional contributions or assessments determined necessary by the 
Employer or Trustees to remedy any shortage in the Trust Fund to pay Plan Benefits and Plan 
expenses or to fund a trust reserve for payment of future Plan Benefits and Plan expenses.  All 
contributions received, together with the income therefrom shall be held, invested, and applied 
by the Trustees for the payment of Plan Benefits and Plan expenses pursuant to the terms of this 
Trust Agreement without distinction between principal and income. The Trustees shall not be 
responsible for the collection of any contribution under the Plan, but shall be responsible only for 
property received by them pursuant to this Trust Agreement.  
 
ARTICLE III – PAYMENTS FROM TRUST FUND  
 
 
3.1  Payments Directed by Plan Administrator.  The Trustees shall from time to time, at 
the request of the Plan Administrator, or a Third Party Administrator delegated the responsibility 
to pay claims for Participants’ Plan Benefits, make payments out of the Trust Fund for Plan 
administration expenses and to persons or entities to whom such monies are to be paid for 
Participants’ Plan Benefits.  The payments shall be in such amounts as may be specified by the 
Plan Administrator or the delegated Third Party Administrator.  To the extent permitted by law, 
the Trustees shall be under no liability for any payment made pursuant to the direction of the 
Plan Administrator or the delegated Third Party Administrator.  A Third Party Administrator 
having the ability to make transfers or payments from the Trust Fund for Plan Benefits or Plan 
expenses shall be a Trust fiduciary and shall be properly bonded.  Any payment direction of the 
Plan Administrator or the Third Party Administrator shall constitute a certification by the Plan 
Administrator or the Third Party Administrator that the distribution or payment so directed is one 
which the Plan Administrator or the Third Party Administrator is authorized to direct.  
 
 
3.2   Claims for Plan Benefits.  Claims for Plan Benefits shall be paid from the Trust 
Fund.  Claims shall be submitted as provided in the Plan Document and Summary Plan 
Description.  Any Plan Participant whose claim for Plan Benefits has been denied, in whole or in 
part, by the Plan Administrator or Third Party Administrator may request a review of the adverse 
benefit determination under the procedures provided in the Plan Document and Summary Plan 
Description.    
 
 
3.3  Impossibility of Diversion.  It shall be prohibited at any time for any part of the Trust 
Fund to be used for, or diverted to, purposes other than to provide Plan Benefits authorized under 
the Plan for the exclusive benefit of Plan Participants and for the payment of any taxes and 
administration expenses for which the Trust is liable as provided in this Trust Agreement.    
 

 
 
Page 3 of  9 
 
3.4  Payments for Policies.  The Plan may provide for policies of insurance to be obtained 
with payments from the Trust Fund to provide Plan Benefits.  The Plan provides for an insurance 
policy to insure the Plan and the Trust against losses from excessive claims (“stop loss 
insurance”).  The Trustees shall coordinate with the Plan Administrator to ensure adequate stop 
loss insurance coverage is obtained for the Plan and the Trust.  Stop loss insurance shall be 
owned by and held for the benefit of the Trust Fund.  The Trustees may pay premiums for stop 
loss insurance policies and Plan Benefit insurance policies from the Trust Fund as authorized 
Trust expenses.  
 
 
3.5  Administrative Expenses.  The Employer or Plan Administrator may need to hire: 
third party administrators, consultants, legal counsel, accountants, auditors, and administrative 
support services to provide administrative claims payment services and to assist the Plan 
Administrator in making the Plan available to the Employer’s eligible employees and their 
dependents; maintaining Plan records; enrolling eligible employees and dependents; and 
performing other Plan administration duties.  The Trustees may pay the charges for such 
administrative support services at the direction of the Plan Administrator and may rely upon the 
Plan Administrator’s determination that such services are reasonable Plan administration 
expenses.    
 
ARTICLE IV – INVESTMENTS  
 
 
4.1  General.  The Trustees shall invest and reinvest the principal and income of the Trust 
Fund and keep the Trust Fund invested, without distinction between principal and income, in 
such securities or in such property, real or personal, tangible or intangible, as the Trustees shall 
deem advisable, as outlined in the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming Investment Policy Statement. 
 
 
4.2  Establishment of Funding Policy. The Trustees and Plan Administrator shall 
establish and carry out a funding policy consistent with the purposes of the Plan and the 
requirements of applicable law.  As part of such funding policy, the Plan Administrator shall 
from time to time direct the Trustees to exercise their investment discretion so as to provide 
sufficient cash assets in an amount determined by the Plan Administrator, under the funding 
policy then in effect, to be necessary to meet the liquidity requirements for the payment of Plan 
Benefits and Plan Expenses.  The Trustees shall be excluded fiduciaries under the Wyoming 
Uniform Trust Code with respect to any investment made pursuant to the direction of the Plan 
Administrator. 
 
 
4.3  Trustees’ Adherence to Funding Policy.  The Trustees’ discretion in investing and 
reinvesting the principal and income of the Trust Fund shall be subject to the funding policy, and 
any changes to the policy that the Plan Administrator and Trustees may adopt, pursuant to Plan 
needs, from time to time.  The Trustees shall have the duty to act strictly in accordance with such 
funding policy, and any changes therein, as agreed to by the Trustees and Plan Administrator.  
 
ARTICLE V – POWERS OF TRUSTEES  
 
 
5.1  Powers.  The Trustees, in addition to all powers and authorities under the Wyoming 

 
 
Page 4 of  9 
Uniform Trust Code and provisions of this Trust Agreement, shall have the following powers 
and authorities, to be exercised in the Trustees’ sole discretion:  
 
(a) To borrow or raise money for the purposes of the Trust in such amount, and upon such terms 
and conditions, as the Trustees shall deem advisable; and for any sum so borrowed, to issue a 
promissory note as Trustees, and to secure the repayment thereof by pledging all, or any part, of 
the Trust Fund; and no person lending money to the Trustees shall be bound to see to the 
application of the money lent or to inquire into the validity, expediency, or propriety of any 
borrowing; 
 
(b) To keep such portion of the Trust Fund in cash or cash balances as the Trustees may, from 
time to time, deem to be in the best interests of the trust created hereby, without liability for 
interest thereon; 
 
(c) To accept and retain for such time as they may deem advisable any securities or other 
property received or acquired as Trustees hereunder, whether or not such securities or other 
property would normally be purchased as investments hereunder;  
 
(d) To make, execute, acknowledge, and deliver any and all documents of transfer and 
conveyance and any and all other instruments that may be necessary or appropriate to carry out 
the powers herein granted;  
 
(e) To settle, compromise, or submit to arbitration any claims, debts, or damages to or owing to 
or from the Trust Fund, to commence or defend suits or legal or administrative proceedings, and 
to represent the Trust Fund in all suits and legal and administrative proceedings;  
 
(f) To employ suitable agents and counsel and to pay their reasonable expenses and 
compensation, such agents or counsel may or may not be agents or counsel for the Employer;  
 
(g) To invest in Treasury Bills and other forms of United States government obligations as 
allowed under the City of Cheyenne’s Investment Policy Statement; 
 
(h) To deposit monies in federally insured savings accounts or certificates of deposit in banks or 
savings and loan associations as allowed under the City of Cheyenne’s Investment Policy 
Statement;  
 
(i) To request Employer pay to the Trust additional contributions or assessments when such are 
found necessary by the Trustees to remedy any shortage in the Trust Fund to pay Plan Benefits 
and Plan expenses or to fund a trust reserve for payment of future Plan Benefits and Plan 
expenses;   
 
(j) To do all such acts, take all such proceedings, and exercise all such rights and privileges, 
although not specifically mentioned herein, as the Trustees may deem necessary to administer 
the Trust Fund, and to carry out the purposes of this Trust. 
 

 
 
Page 5 of  9 
5.2  Taxes and Expenses.  An individual serving as Trustee who already receives full-time pay 
from the Employer shall not receive compensation from this Trust except for reimbursement of 
expenses properly and actually incurred.  The Trustees shall be reimbursed for any reasonable 
expenses, including reasonable counsel and accounting fees, incurred by the Trustees in the 
administration of the Trust Fund.  Such expenses shall be paid from the Trust Fund.  All taxes of 
any kind and all kinds whatsoever that may be levied or assessed under existing or future laws 
upon, or in respect of, the Trust Fund or the income thereof shall be paid by the Trustees from 
the Trust Fund. 
 
5.3  Prohibited Transactions.  The Trustees, Employer, Plan Administrator, and any Plan 
Participants may not, directly or indirectly, accept any compensation, gifts, commissions, or 
emoluments whatsoever (collectively, "compensation") in connection with or on account of any 
transaction between the Trustees and any third party administrator providing services to the Plan 
or an issuer of a Policy or Policies obtained with payments from the Trust Fund to provide Plan 
Benefits or to insure the Plan.  
 
ARTICLE VI –  TRUSTEES' DUTIES AND ACTIONS  
 
6.1  General.  The Trustees shall, consistent with the Trust funding policy, invest, manage, and 
control the Trust Fund.  The Trustees shall discharge their duties under this Trust Agreement 
solely in the interest of the Plan Participants and for the exclusive purpose of providing Plan 
Benefits to the Plan Participants and defraying reasonable expenses of administering the Trust.  
The Trustees shall discharge their duties with the care, skill, prudence and diligence under the 
circumstances then prevailing that a prudent man acting in a like capacity and familiar with such 
matters would use in the conduct of an enterprise of a like character and with like aims.  The 
Trustees shall diversify the investments of the Trust so as to minimize the risk of large losses, 
unless under the circumstances it is clearly prudent not to do so.  The Trustees’ duties shall be 
performed in accordance with the provisions of this Trust Agreement insofar as they are 
consistent with the Wyoming Uniform Trust Code, as this Trust Agreement and the Trust Code 
may be from time to time amended.    
 
6.2  Actions of Trustees.  An action of the Trustees shall be by a majority of their number.  The 
Trustees may delegate authority for one or more of them to sign documents, instruments, 
including borrowing instruments, and checks or other payment authorizations on the Trust’s 
behalf.   
 
6.3  Consultation and Indemnification. The Trustees may consult with counsel and the Trustees 
shall not be deemed imprudent by reason of taking or refraining from taking any action in 
accordance with the opinion of counsel.  The Employer agrees, to the extent permitted by law, to 
indemnify and hold the Trustees harmless from and against any liability that the Trustees may 
incur in the administration of the Trust Fund, unless arising from the Trustees’ willful breach of 
the provisions of this Trust Agreement.  The Employer may require the Trustees to obtain a 
fidelity bond or fiduciary liability insurance that would cover Trustees of a welfare benefit fund 
held in trust, and the Trustees may pay for the same out the Trust Fund.    
 

 
 
Page 6 of  9 
6.4  Accounts, Records, and Reporting. The Trustees shall keep accurate and detailed accounts of 
all investments, receipts, disbursements, and other transactions hereunder, and all such accounts 
and other records relating thereto shall be open to inspection and audit at all reasonable times by 
any person designated by the Plan Administrator or the Employer.  The Trustees shall furnish to 
the Plan Administrator a written statement of account within 60 days after the end of the Trust’s 
fiscal year.  The written statement of account shall set forth all receipts and disbursements, 
additions, deductions, assets, liabilities, and net assets of the Trust Fund in accordance with 
applicable accounting standards.  The Plan Administrator shall acknowledge receipt of the 
written statement of account and advise the Trustees of his or her approval or disapproval of the 
statement of account.  Failure by the Plan Administrator to disapprove any such statement of 
account within 30 days after its receipt shall be deemed approval of the account.  To the extent 
permitted by applicable law, the approval or deemed approval by the Plan Administrator of the 
statement of account shall serve to release and discharge the Trustees from any liability or 
accountability to the Employer as respects the propriety of the Trustees’ acts or transactions 
shown in the statement of account.  To the extent any reports to the Internal Revenue Service,  
United States Department of Labor, or other agency concerning the Trust Fund are required by 
applicable law, the Trustees shall provide the Plan Administrator with the information required 
to complete the reports.   
 
6.5  Record Retention.  The Trustees shall retain records relating to the Trust Fund for as long as 
necessary for the proper administration thereof and at least for any period required by applicable 
law.   
 
6.6  Limitation on Trustees’ Liability.  The Plan Administrator and any delegated Third Party 
Administrator shall administer the Plan as provided in the Plan Document and Summary Plan 
Description and this Trust, and the Trustees shall not be responsible in any respect for 
administering the Plan nor shall the Trustees be responsible for the adequacy of contributions to 
the Trust Fund to meet or discharge any payments for Plan Benefits, Plan expenses, Plan 
reserves, or for any other Plan liabilities.  The Trustees shall be entitled conclusively to rely upon 
any notice, instruction, direction or other communication of the Plan Administrator.   
 
ARTICLE VII – RESIGNATION, REMOVAL AND REPLACEMENT OF TRUSTEES  
 
7.1  Appointment of Trustees.  This Trust shall be administered by Trustees appointed by the 
Employer.  The initial Trustees appointed by Employer are the Trustees signing this Trust 
Agreement.     
 
7.2  Resignation. A Trustee may resign at any time by giving 30 days' notice in writing to the 
Employer and Plan Administrator.  
 
7.3  Removal. The Employer may remove a Trustee at any time upon 30 days' notice in writing 
to the Trustee.  
 
7.4  Successor Trustees. Upon the resignation or removal of a Trustee, the Employer shall 
appoint a successor trustee who shall have the same powers and duties as those conferred upon 

 
 
Page 7 of  9 
the Trustees in this Trust Agreement.  If all Trustees were to resign or be removed, upon 
acceptance of appointment by successor trustees, the Trustees shall assign, transfer, and pay over 
to the successor trustees the funds and properties then constituting the Trust Fund.  Resigning or 
removed Trustees are authorized, however, to reserve such reasonable sum of money, as it may 
deem advisable, for payment of fees and expenses in connection with the settlement of their 
accounts or otherwise, and any balance of such reserve remaining after the payment of such fees 
and expenses shall be paid over to the successor trustees.  Any successor trustees must sign this 
Trust Agreement or another document agreeing to be bound by this Trust Agreement.   
 
7.5  Report by Trustees. Within 60 days after the resignation or removal of all the Trustees, the 
Trustees shall furnish to the Employer a written statement of account with respect to the portion 
of the year for which the Trustees served. The Employer shall acknowledge receipt of the 
statement in writing and advise the Trustees of the approval or disapproval of the statement.  
Failure by the Employer to disapprove any such statement of account within 30 days after its 
receipt shall be deemed approval of the statement.  The approval by the Employer of the 
statement of account shall serve to release and discharge the resigning or removed Trustees from 
any liability or accountability to the Employer as respects the propriety of the Trustees’ acts or 
transactions shown in the statement of account.  
 
7.6  Waiver of Notice. In the event of any resignation or removal of a Trustee, the Trustee and 
the Employer may in writing waive any notice of resignation or removal as may be provided in 
this Trust Agreement.   
  
ARTICLE VIII – AMENDMENT AND TERMINATION OF TRUST AGREEMENT  
 
8.1  Amendment.  Any or all of the provisions of this Trust Agreement may be amended at any 
time and from time to time, in whole or in part, by the Employer and the Trustees by an 
instrument in writing. No such amendment shall authorize or permit any part of the Trust Fund 
(other than such part as is required to pay taxes and administration expenses) to be used for or 
diverted to purposes other than for the exclusive benefit of Plan Participants; and no such 
amendment shall cause or permit any portion of the Trust Fund to revert to or become the 
property of the Employer.  
 
8.2  Termination.  This Trust Agreement may be terminated at any time by the Employer, and 
upon such termination, or upon the dissolution or liquidation of the Employer, the Trust Fund 
shall be paid out by the Trustees as and when directed by the Plan Administrator or the 
Employer, in accordance with the provisions of Article III of this Trust Agreement and the terms 
of the Plan.  In no event, may any amount of the Trust Fund be returned to the Employer except 
to be used to provide for a health insurance plan providing benefits for Employer’s employees 
and the employee’s dependents that are similar to the Plan Benefits funded under this Trust 
Agreement. 
 
ARTICLE IX – GENERAL  
 
9.1  Source of Funds. This Trust shall constitute the sole source of funds which may be used to 

 
 
Page 8 of  9 
pay Plan Benefits, and the Employer shall not be liable in any way or in any manner for any such 
Plan Benefits or payments beyond those monies which are to be contributed to this Trust as 
provided under Article II of this Trust Agreement.   
 
9.2  Limited Effect of Plan and Trust.  Neither the establishment of the Plan nor the Trust nor 
any modification thereof, nor the creation of any fund or account, nor the payment of any Plan 
Benefits, shall be construed as giving to any Plan Participant or other person any legal or 
equitable right against the Trustees, the Employer, or any officer or employee thereof, except as 
may otherwise be provided in the Plan or in this Trust Agreement. Under no circumstances shall 
the term of employment of any of the Employer’s employees be modified or in any way affected 
by the Plan or this Trust Agreement.  
 
9.3  Nonalienation of Benefits.  Neither the Employer, Trustees, any Plan Participant, nor any 
other person shall have any rights, title, or interest in the Trust Fund other than as specifically 
provided in the Plan and this Trust Agreement.  No Plan Participant shall have the option to 
receive any part of the contributions to the Trust Fund in lieu of Plan Benefits.  Neither the Trust 
Fund nor any contribution to it shall in any manner be liable for or subject to the debts, contracts, 
or liabilities of the Employer, Trustees, Plan Administrator, Third Party Administrator, or any 
Plan Participant.  No part of the Trust Fund nor any benefit payable in accordance with the Plan 
shall be subject in any manner to anticipation, alienation, sale, transfer, assignment, pledge, 
encumbrance, or charge; provided, however, that these restrictions shall not prohibit the Plan 
Administrator from establishing a procedure whereby a Plan Participant may direct the Plan 
Benefits due him or her to be paid to the doctor, hospital, or other person or institution from 
which he or she received medical, hospital, or other services for which Plan Benefits are due. 
 
9.4  Protective Clause.  Neither the Employer nor the Trustees shall be responsible for the 
validity of any contract of insurance issued in connection with the Plan or Trust or for the failure 
on the part of the insurer to make payments provided by such contract, or for the action of any 
person which may delay payment or render a contract null and void or unenforceable in whole or 
in part.  
 
9.5  Compliance with Laws.  The Plan, this Trust Agreement and the Trust Fund are intended to 
meet the requirements for a welfare benefit fund with welfare benefit fund assets held in a trust. 
 
9.6  Construction of Trust Agreement. This Trust Agreement shall be construed and enforced 
according to the laws of Wyoming.  If any provision of this Trust Agreement shall be held illegal 
or invalid for any reason, such determination shall not affect the remaining provisions of the 
Trust Agreement.  
 
9.7  Gender and Number. Wherever any words are used herein in the masculine, feminine or 
neuter, they shall be construed as though they were also used in another gender in all cases where 
they would so apply, and whenever any words are used herein in the singular or plural form, they 
shall be construed as though they were also used in the other form in all cases where they would 
so apply.

convenience of reference and are to be ignored in any construction of the provisions hereof.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-04-25_TotalExtraction_31901668.pdf
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
MEMORANDUM 
 
DATE:  
April 16, 2022 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick J. Collins 
 
 
Members of the City Council 
 
 
FROM:  
Thomas D. Cobb, PE, City Engineer 
 
 
SUBJECT: 
A RESOLUTION ACCEPTING A U.S. DEPARTMENT OF HOMELAND SECURITY 
FEDERAL EMERGENCY MANAGEMENT AGENCY FISCAL YEAR 2020 BUILDING 
RESILIENT INFRASTRUCTURE AND COMMUNITIES GRANT, IN THE AMOUNT OF 
$150,000, FOR DRY CREEK RAIL CROSSING PLANNING AND DESIGN WORK 
 
 
Please find the attached Resolution for acceptance of a $150,000 Grant Award from Homeland 
Security initiated by the City Engineering Department.  
 
Background:  
 
The Cheyenne Metropolitan Planning Organization (MPO) recently conducted a plan of East 
Park which identified needed improvements to the drainage conveyance of the Dry Creek basin 
thru the Union Pacific Railroad embankment in the vicinity of milepost 504.75, Sidney Sub.  
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
A COMMUNITY OF CHOICE 
OFFICE OF THE CITY ENGINEER 
2101 O’Neil Avenue Room 206  
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
Phone (307)637-6268    Fax (307)637-6256 

Memorandum 
Honorable Mayor Patrick J. Collins 
Members of City Council 
 
 
April 16, 2022 
Page 2 of 4 
 
 
Currently drainage flows thru the UPRR embankment via a 
historic 6’ wide x 8’ tall box arch masonry structure built by the 
UPRR in 1903. 
 
The Dry Creek drainage basin contributing flow upstream of the 
UPRR culvert is approximately 11 square miles in size.  The 
existing conditions are such that during a large storm event, 
stormwater is held up against the UPRR Embankment until it can 
flow thru the masonry structure.  The existing railroad 
embankment is approximately 25’ tall in the vicinity of this arch 
structure.   The image below indicates the FEMA 100-Year 
Floodplain and Floodway in the vicinity of this arch structure (the 
red line in the image.) 
 
 
 
 

Memorandum 
Honorable Mayor Patrick J. Collins 
Members of City Council 
 
 
April 16, 2022 
Page 3 of 4 
 
 
 
 
The consultant has completed a preliminary hydraulic study and has made recommendations 
for improvements to this area known as East Park.  The preliminary recommendations are 
summarized below and will be finalized with this design. 
 
1. Construct an engineered berm north of the existing UPRR embankment. This additional 
berm will protect the existing railroad embankment by removing storm and flood waters 
and eliminating pooling and erosion of the existing railroad embankment. 
2. Install 3 – 60” borings thru the existing UPRR embankment and the constructed 
engineered berm referenced in Item No. 1 above, following all AREMA Guidelines. These 
three pipes will convey the storm water from East Park thru the embankment to 
continue flowing in Dry Creek south of the embankment. 
3. Install 1 – 54” boring thru the existing UPRR embankment near the existing arch 
structure also following all AREMA Guidelines.  This 54” boring will convey storm water 
that is currently flowing along the UPRR embankment from the west and east, directing 
it to the south into Dry Creek on the south side of the UPRR embankment. 

Memorandum 
Honorable Mayor Patrick J. Collins 
Members of City Council 
 
 
April 16, 2022 
Page 4 of 4 
 
 
Reason for Grant: 
 
Over the last 120-years, the Dry Creek basin has experienced 100-year or larger flood events at 
least seven times.  Four of those resulted in significant impounding of water against the UPRR 
embankment referenced in this letter.  There have been at least four occasions where as much 
as 19-feet of stormwater has been impounded against the UPRR embankment since the 6’ x 8’ 
box arch culvert was constructed in 1903.  During the last major event in 1985, the 6’ x 8’ box 
arch culvert was partially blocked with flood debris.  On average, our community experiences a 
large magnitude event in the Dry Creek drainage basin about every twenty years.  It is not a 
matter of if but when the next big flood event will occur in the basin. We have not experienced 
that level of storm in some time.    
 
Clearly the recommended improvements provide protection of UPRR facilities and removes the 
primary Dry Creek Basin storm water from utilizing the historic box arch structure.   We believe 
these recommendations provide direct benefit to the UPRR and an opportunity for the City of 
Cheyenne to potentially use the historic box arch structure as a pedestrian underpass for the 
Cheyenne Greenway system.   
 
Cost and Funding: 
 
The total cost of project to the City of Cheyenne shall not exceed $50,000.  The funding for the 
project is the 2019-2022 5th Penny 1% Sales Tax dedicated Drainage Fund. 

RESOLUTION NO. ________ 
  
 
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION ACCEPTING A U.S. DEPARTMENT OF HOMELAND 
SECURITY 
FEDERAL 
EMERGENCY 
MANAGEMENT 
AGENCY 
FISCAL YEAR 2020 BUILDING RESILIENT INFRASTRUCTURE AND 
COMMUNITIES GRANT.”  
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne applied for and has been awarded a grant of $150,000 
from the Wyoming Homeland Security Office for project scoping design work for the Cheyenne 
Dry Creek Union Pacific Railroad Crossing; and 
 
WHEREAS, the required match of $50,000 will be provided through 2019 to 2022 5th 
Penny Sales Tax administered by the City Engineer Department; and  
 
WHEREAS, the period of performance for the grant is December 30, 2021 through 
October 31, 2024; and 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne will procure a professional consultant to collect and 
evaluate pertinent data needed for hydraulic modeling of alternative conveyance pipes thru the 
Union Pacific Railroad embankment in the vicinity of milepost 504.75, Sidney Sub., on the Dry 
Creek drainage basin and evaluate/ design a detention storage facility separated from the UPRR 
embankment. Preparation of Cost Estimates/ Benefit Cost Analysis (BCA) will be prepared along 
with construction plans and provide to the City of Cheyenne.  The intent of the planning and 
design efforts is to produce an eligible application-ready construction project for consideration by 
FEMA for future BRIC program funding. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming is in support of this project and of the acceptance of this grant; and that the Mayor is 
authorized to execute a grant agreement to receive this grant funding for this drainage planning 
project. 
 
PRESENTED, READ, AND ADOPTED THIS ______ DAY OF MAY 2022. 
 
 
____________________________________ 
Patrick J. Collins, Mayor

Community Information System (CIS) database. 
 
Please provide any additional comments below (optional). 
 
The City of Cheyenne, Wyoming is applying for FY2020 Building Resilient Infrastructure and Communities (BRIC) Project Scoping funding under the Capability-and 
Capacity- Building (C&CB) Activity for funding assistance for a project scoping/design effort in the lower Dry Creek drainage basin. Our proposed project scoping 
effort/design will evaluate and identify a feasible, constructible boring location under the Union Pacific Railroad main line tracks entering into the eastern corporate 
limits of Cheyenne. This project scoping effort will address a critical flood hazard for the community and result in an application-ready construction project for a 
future BRIC funding opportunity in FY2022. The City of Cheyenne, established in 1867 as a railhead along the Union Pacific Railroad, was built along Crow Creek. 
As Cheyenne grew, its growth occurred away from Crow Creek and along the nine tributaries within the corporate limits including the Dry Creek drainage basin. 
Dry Creek is a tributary of Crow Creek with a drainage area of 14.6 sq. miles. The headwaters originate on Francis E. Warren AFB. The north and south forks of Dry 
Creek flow through the Western Hills subdivision and form a confluence into the main branch just east of I-25 on School District property. Much of the creek has 
been infringed upon as the community developed resulting in loss of floodplain and increasing drainage problems. In the community’s 1988 Dry Creek Drainage 
Master Plan, flood problems were classified as life-safety hazards and/or property damage hazards. Commercial and residential areas upstream of the Union 
Pacific Railroad (UPRR) embankment in the lower basin was classified as a high life-safety hazard area. So was Campstool Road, downstream of I-80. Since 1988, 
commercial and residential development has grown exponentially both upstream and downstream of the UPRR and, the channel downstream of the Campstool Rd. 
crossing, between Campstool Rd. and I-80, has continued to degrade. An ongoing head cut is affecting the Campstool Rd. crossing, approximately 1000-feet 
downstream of the UPRR. Floods with the highest peak discharges affecting Cheyenne are the result of intense convective rainstorms occurring over the 
community. The earliest flood of record was in 1883 and, the most devastating occurred in 1985. The 1985 event produced record peak discharges throughout the 
Dry Creek basin resulting in overtopping of every road crossing and multiple deaths along the Sheridan Reach of Dry Creek. At the UPRR, the ensuing floodwaters 
became ponded to a depth of 20-feet behind the 25-foot high railroad embankment, nearly overtopping it. The ponded waters inundated rural residents and a 
commercial property along Whitney Rd. the discharge through the undersized UPRR culvert spread out downstream, overtopping Campstool Rd. at the confluence 
with Crow Creek. Floods of the 1985 magnitude have occurred in the lower Dry Creek basin in 1918, 1935, and 1955. The 1955 event produced similar depths of 
ponding to the 1985 flood elevation at the UPRR embankment. The UPRR culvert was originally constructed in 1903 and is severely undersized to convey much 
more than a 10-year flood event. The embankment begins to backup and pond water beginning with a 10-year event. The City is experiencing significant growth in 
the lower Dry Creek basin including continued commercial growth in the LEEDS business park, immediately downstream of the UPRR. The continued potential for 
significant ponding at the UPRR embankment is a dangerous situation that impacts development in the lower Dry Creek basin and presents a critical life-safety 
hazard for businesses and traveling public downstream of the UPRR. The City of Cheyenne is pursuing the development of a feasible and constructible boring 
under the UPRR along with a re-designed detention storage area away from the UPRR embankment to eliminate the existing life-safety hazard and reduce property 
damage due to flooding.

Mitigation plan 
 
 
Please provide your plan information below. 
 
Is the entity that will benefit from the proposed activity covered by the current FEMA approved multi-hazard mitigation plan in compliance with 44 CFR 
Part 201? Yes 
 
Please provide plan detail 
 
Plan name 
 
Wyoming Region 7 Hazard Mitigation Plan 
 
Plan type 
 
Local Multijurisdictional Multi-Hazard Mitigation Plan 
 
Plan approval date 
 
08/01/2017 
 
Proposed activity description 
 
The City of Cheyenne is part of the 2017 Wyoming Region 7 Multi-Jurisdictional Hazard Mitigation Plan. The mitigation plan for the City includes mitigation 
strategies for flood control projects including those planned for the Dry Creek Basin to mitigate flood hazards. Our proposed project scoping activity will address a 
critical life-safety and property damage hazard in the lower Dry Creek basin. The recently awarded advance assistance project application to the City of Cheyenne 
under DR-4327 HMGP (June 2019 – project 4R) will allow the City to update the Hydrologic model for the Dry Creek Drainage Basin with an updated Master 
Drainage Plan and a suite of recommended mitigation strategies. The hydrologic conditions at the UPRR crossing are not likely to have significant changes with 
the Master Plan update. Additional culverts in conjunction with the construction of detention storage removed from the Union Pacific embankment should certainly 
be included in mitigation strategies coming out of the Master Drainage Plan efforts. The project scope for the Dry Creek Drainage Master Plan, located within the 
City and County Limits; starting upstream, (west) at the watershed boundary with F.E. Warren AFB, and ending at the Crow Creek confluence, east of town would 
incorporate the Union Pacific main line tracks in the lower area of the basin. The Master Plan may be completed by Fall, 2021 and hydrologic results incorporated 
into our proposed Dry Creek Project Scoping effort, providing an added level of accuracy. For the purposes of our proposed Project Scoping effort, the hydraulics 
will be completely updated based on the existing, regulatory hydrologic model. This approach will yield a high- quality deliverable and a solid basis for a feasible 
and constructible project as the hydrologic model input for the UPRR subbasin shouldn't change significantly with the overall basin update. The C&CB funding will 
assist the City of Cheyenne in developing an effective mitigation strategy that will have a significant impact on public safety along with a meaningful reduction in 
potential flooding of commercial and residential structures. Please provide any additional comments below (optional). 
 
Both the 1988 Dry Creek Drainage Master Plan and the 2000 Surface Water Drainage Committee Final Report recommended construction of a flood 
berm to protect residential properties from flooding upstream of the UPRR. Beginning with the 2004 Storm Water Utility Ballot Initiative, there have 
been recommendations from the City Engineer’s Office and local engineers for the construction of more efficient conveyance pipes under the UPRR 
along with construction of stormwater detention away from the UPRR embankment. The City has recently purchased the sump area on the upstream 
side of the UPRR embankment for use as a future community park for east side residents. The City is now well positioned to design and construct a 
new flood control facility to address these critical flood hazards and potentially provide multiple benefits to community residents in the process. Our 
project scoping efforts under the C&CB activity will tie into past master drainage plan recommendations, as well as, compliment the ongoing advance 
assistance project efforts under the recently awarded DR-4327 HMGP (June 2019 – project 4R) funding. Our project scoping/design efforts for the 
UPRR flood control project will lead to an application-ready mitigation project for a future BRIC funding opportunity in FY2022 and, represents a 
worthwhile investment of BRIC funding as we are addressing multiple community lifelines with this project. 
 
Scope of work 
 
 
The project Scope of Work (SOW) identifies the eligible activity, describes what will be accomplished and explains how the mitigation activity will be 
implemented. The mitigation activity must be described in sufficient detail to verify the cost estimate. All activities for which funding is requested must be 
identified in the SOW prior to the close of the application period. FEMA has different requirements for project, planning and management cost SOWs. 
 
Subapplication title (include type of activity and location) 
 
Cheyenne Dry Creek UPRR Crossing Project Scoping Design 
 
Activities 
 
Primary activity type 
 
Develop or conduct engineering, environmental, feasibility and/or benefit cost analyses 
 
Secondary activity type (Optional) 
 
Activities supporting development of applications 
 
Tertiary activity type (Optional) 
 
Evaluate facilities to identify mitigation actions 
 
Geographic areas description 
 
The project scoping activity is located on the eastern side of Cheyenne, Wyoming where the UPRR crosses Dry Creek, approximately 
3000-feet upstream of the confluence of Dry Creek with Crow Creek.

How will the mitigation activity be implemented? 
 
The City of Cheyenne City Engineer’s Office will manage the procurement of a qualified consultant; coordination and supervision of the 
consultant’s efforts; close out and, in conjunction with the City Treasurer staff, project reporting and invoicing. 
 
What is the scope of work of the proposal? Will it result in a complete project application for future funding opportunities? 
 
Our proposed project scoping scope-of-work will include the following tasks: Field Surveys and Reconnaissance Selected consultant will collect and evaluate 
pertinent data needed for hydraulic modeling of alternative conveyance pipes under the UPRR and, evaluation/design of a detention storage facility separated 
from the UPRR embankment. Topographic Data Development Selected consultant will develop supplemental digital topographic data for use in the hydraulic and 
geotechnical modeling efforts. Hydraulic Analysis Selected consultant will develop a working hydraulic model to evaluate and design alternative conveyance 
pipes in conjunction with a revised upstream storage and potential downstream storage elements using a reach specific model. The model will incorporate the 
Environmental Protection Agency SWMM model and the CORPS’ HEC-RAS model. Consultant will supplement these models with a specific culvert analysis model 
if necessary. The underlying hydrologic input will be taken from the regulatory hydrologic model with a QA/QC conducted using the updated hydrologic data from 
the ongoing Dry Creek master plan update when it becomes available. Preparation of Cost Estimates/BCA Development Selected consultant will prepare 
construction plans and provide the City with engineer’s estimates for construction cost. Consultant will conduct BCA of each alternative hydraulic analysis of 
potential mitigation strategies/ projects ensuring the constructability and cost-effectiveness of the recommended strategy. This should result in an eligible 
application-ready construction project for consideration by FEMA for FY2022 BRIC program funding. 
 
Who will manage and complete the mitigation activity? 
 
The City of Cheyenne City Engineer’s Office, in conjunction with City Treasurer staff and a qualified consultant, will manage and administer this project scoping 
effort. City Engineer Office staff has experience and the necessary expertise to manage a project of this size and complexity, as well as coordinate and assist with 
additional public outreach if appropriate. A qualified consultant will be hired through a competitive procurement process to execute the project scoping tasks. The 
consultant will also assist City staff in developing an application for FY2022 BRIC program funding for a construction project, if eligible. 
 
What are you doing to consider other risks in the project area? 
 
The City’s ongoing Dry Creek Master Drainage Plan Update is the first step in developing mitigation strategies/projects for reducing/eliminating the 
existing flood hazards for the lower Dry Creek Basin. Development of the drainage strategies in the Master Plan Update will complement our proposed 
project scoping effort for the UPRR embankment/flood hazard and provide a reduction to property damages due to flooding and an elimination of 
critical life-safety hazards due to flooding. Both the Dry Creek Master Drainage Plan Update and our proposed project scoping effort are 
implementations of prior recommendations from master plans, the City Engineer’s Office, and local engineers to address the existing, critical flood 
hazards facing residents, businesses, and the traveling public in the lower Dry Creek Basin. 
 
Is there an estimate for when the mitigation activity will take place? 
 
The City of Cheyenne will initiate the Dry Creek UPRR crossing project scoping design efforts funded under the C&CB activities in August 2021. The City is 
desiring to have a construction project grant ready for submission to FEMA’s BRIC program in the fall of 2022. Construction would begin in 2023. 
 
What alternatives will be considered? 
 
The selected consultant will use an iterative approach with the hydraulic modeling of project scoping alternative designs, resulting in 
numerous alternatives to be vetted with the community and evaluated by qualified engineers through the course of the project scoping 
design efforts. This approach will lead to a cost-effective project recommendation for the community. 
 
Do activities being considered align with hazard mitigation plan? 

Attachment A 
Subgrant Project Scoping Application 
Attachment A to the Grant Award Agreement between Wyoming Office of Homeland Security and the City of Cheyenne 
Page 5 of 14 
 
The project scoping efforts proposed for the study basin are aligned with hazards addressed in the Wyoming Region 7 Multi-Jurisdictional 
Hazards Plan. Our proposed project scoping efforts will lead to a mitigation project that significantly reduces the existing property damage 
hazard due to flooding and, eliminates the existing life-safety hazard due to potential breaching of a high embankment.

Please explain why this project is not applicable. 
 
This is a Project Scoping effort to develop an eligible construction project for BRIC funding consideration. 
 
Please provide any additional comments below (optional). 
 
The City of Cheyenne is pursuing C&CB Project Scoping funding to help fund a project scoping/design effort in the lower Dry Creek Basin. The City intends on 
evaluating alternative designs to determine a feasible, constructible boring under the Union Pacific main line tracks for new culverts/pipes. A detailed Hydraulic 
model will be developed along with a geotechnical analysis to base the design upon. The completed design will provide the community with a construction-ready 
BRIC Mitigation Project for the FY2022 grant cycle. The existing conditions for flow in Dry Creek at the UPRR crossing present a significant hazard with respect to 
compromise of the 25-foot tall railroad embankment by flood waters. The design effort will include evaluation of downstream conditions along with proposed 
mitigation for any impacts caused by the new culverts. There is a unique opportunity to partner with the Laramie County Conservation District to provide 
environmental enhancements on their headquarters property located immediately downstream of the UPRR embankment. Additional discharge downstream would 
create more sustainable conditions for additional wetlands, meeting their mission statement for their headquarters site. Additional storage, removed from the 
UPRR embankment, along with increased conveyance downstream would address the current noncompliant situation of using the railroad embankment as a dam. 
In the 1985 flood event, the railroad embankment impounded stormwater to a depth of 20-feet. Our project scoping design effort would eliminate this unsafe 
condition and provide restoration of wetlands both upstream and downstream of the UPRR crossing. Results from our master drainage planning will result in a 
construction-ready mitigation grant for consideration in the FY2022 BRIC program.

Describe involvement of partners to enhance the mitigation activity outcome. 
 
The City has had active public outreach processes for numerous projects and planning efforts. They will be conducting an extensive public outreach process for 
the ongoing Dry Creek Master Drainage Plan Update as well as one for the concurrent MPO planning effort for the newly created East Park, immediately upstream 
of the UPRR crossing of Dry Creek. They have coordinated and closely partnered with state and federal entities local project stakeholders. The Town is requesting 
a C&CB project scoping investment of $150,000 (75% federal share) for cost sharing for the lower Dry Creek UPRR Crossing Project Scoping Design effort. The 
City’s 25% share of the project scoping effort ($50,000) will be 100% sourced through our dedicated 1% Drainage Fund. 

Attachment A 
Subgrant Project Scoping Application 
Attachment A to the Grant Award Agreement between Wyoming Office of Homeland Security and the City of Cheyenne 
Page 10 of 14 
 
Additional comments (optional) 
 
The Cheyenne community has experienced exponential growth, both residential and commercial, in the lower Dry Creek Basin in recent years. The City is now 
proactively attempting to address long-standing critical flood hazards that pose significant life-safety and property damage hazards to the community and impact 
continued economic development in the basin. The City is pursuing an update of the Dry Creek drainage master plan including a high-level SWMM model and 
underlying GIS capability to better understand, prioritize, and ultimately address the critical drainage issues that have impacted the community. Concurrently, the 
City is pursuing a drainage improvement for the UPRR crossing of Dry Creek to address a critical life-safety hazard affecting residential and commercial properties 
as well as the traveling public. The City has experienced flooding in the lower Dry Creek Basin in the years since the 1985 flood event. With every passing year, the 
probability of another event similar in magnitude to the 1985 event only increases. The potential for a catastrophic breach of the UPRR embankment due to 
ponding of stormwater is a real concern for the community. The potential for property damages due to the existing condition of ponded stormwater has increased 
with the additional development that has occurred in the lower basin. The Federal Emergency Management Agency’s investment will provide the City of Cheyenne 
the opportunity to identify and design the most cost-effective flood control solution for this reach of Dry Creek and reduce the existing risk of flooding within our 
community. The development of a detailed project scoping design is the first step towards achieving that goal.

wide Requirements for Drug-Free Workplace (Grants)." The certifications shall be treated as a material representation of fact upon which reliance 
will be placed when the Federal Emergency Management Agency (FEMA) determines to award the transaction, grant, or cooperative agreement.

Project Scoping 
 
Environmental Historic Preservation Memorandum 
 
EMD-2020-BR-099-0004 
 
The City of Cheyenne will use BRIC funds to select a consultant to evaluate pertinent data needed for 
hydraulic modeling of alternative conveyance pipes under the UPRR and, evaluation/design of a detention 
storage facility separated from the UPRR embankment. The City’s consultant will conduct a BCA of each 
alternative hydraulic analysis of potential mitigation strategies/ projects ensuring the constructability and cost-
effectiveness of the recommended strategy. FEMA Environmental Historic Preservation (EHP) has reviewed 
the project; although there is no information necessary for EHP review at this time since the grant is for project 
scoping activities only, EHP recommends that the City consider the National Environmental Policy Act 
applicable laws and regulations when studying any future project actions. It is recommended that the City start 
EHP existing conditions reviews and analysis once a project area is defined. 
 
This project has potential to have eligible National Register of Historic Places (NRHP) sites within the project 
boundaries. Ground disturbing activities may trigger a Section 106 of the National Historic Preservation Act 
consultation with the Wyoming State Historic Preservation Office. Work within a jurisdictional Waters of the 
U.S. will require a Section 404 permit under the Clean Water Act from the US Army Corps of Engineers and 
may require a floodplain development permit from a local floodplain administrator. Please note that FEMA 
began partial implementation of Federal Flood Risk Management Standard (FFRMS) with FFRMS Interim 
Policy FP-206-21-003 for Hazard Mitigation Assistance Programs. For non-critical actions involving structure 
elevation, dry floodproofing, and mitigation reconstruction in the Special Flood Hazard Area, the policy requires 
that the minimum flood elevation be the base flood elevation plus 2 feet of freeboard (or a higher applicable 
standard), unless doing so would cause the project to be unable to meet cost-effectiveness requirements. 
Additional policies and guidance’s will be forthcoming, and the project team should consider FFRMS in their 
scoping activities, as applicable. 
 
Other environmental laws and regulations that may be applicable to future project planning efforts include: 
Endangered Species Act, Executive Order 11990 (Wetlands), Executive Order 11988 (Floodplain 
Management) and Executive Order 12898: Environmental Justice for Low Income and Minority Populations. 
Upon award of the BRIC project scoping application FEMA EHP staff is available to provide guidance and 
support on NEPA and environmental historic preservation compliance, if requested. Once the project 
boundaries are defined, FEMA EHP recommends a cultural resource and biological resources reports to help 
the City identify any future EHP concerns and/or consultations. These studies can be completed as part of the 
project scoping application scope of work and can streamline the EHP review once the project advances. This 
memorandum and FEMA EHP approval of the requested project scoping funds does not constitute 
authorization of any construction activities associated with proposed future mitigation actions.

Pine Street, 206 Pine Street and 210 Pine Street.  We are currently two 
weeks behind our proposed work schedule due to a limited number of state 
certified appraisers, but believe we can still successfully complete the

Following work performed during this quarter, what percentage of the project 
has been completed?

Advertising and Bids, and 3. Awarding of Contract and 4. Construction 
Mobilization has commenced.  This puts us at 25% of completion of the 
overall project.  We have expended 50% of the budget, however, this is in

document that were met during the quarter; attach copies of permits, if 
applicable.

Coordination with SHPO if any significant finding during construction

Pigeon and coordinate is required with the State Game Fish and Parks to

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-04-25_TotalExtraction_80687598.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. _______ 
ENTITLED:  “A RESOLUTION DECLARING THAT THE MUNICIPALITIES OF 
LARAMIE COUNTY HAVE AGREED THAT THE CURRENT STATE 
STATUTES REGARDING THE DESIGNATION OF LIQUOR LICENSES 
ISSUED BY THE GOVERING BODIES OF SAID MUNICIPALITES ARE 
STIFLING ECONOMIC GROWTH, AND ARE REQUESTING THE 
STATE LEGISLATIVE COMMITTEES AND STATE LEGISLATURE 
REVIEW, CONSIDER, AND ADOPT MODIFICATIONS TO   STATE 
STATUTES REGARDING SAID LICENSES.” 
WHEREAS, the municipalities of Laramie County recognize the need for improvement for 
quality of life throughout Laramie County through existing residents as well as prospective new 
residents. This would include but not limited to our workforce development strategy to encourage 
the residents who reside in Laramie County to stay here for quality of life and not seeking 
entertainment in other locales, and 
WHEREAS, due to recent census data and the current structure of State Statutes on Liquor 
Licenses it has been determined by the municipalities of Laramie County that the State Statutes 
are stifling economic growth in not only large but smaller communities, and  
WHEREAS, the municipalities of Laramie County recognize that tourism is the number 
two industry in Wyoming. The modifications to State Statutes on issuance of liquor licenses for 
new development will create an atmosphere of community and not as a pass through to other 
locales, and 
WHEREAS, the municipalities of Laramie County have recognized the entrepreneurial 
spirit of license applicants, causing the governing bodies to be placed in a precarious situation of 
picking “winners and losers” amongst a variety of qualified candidates. The municipalities of 
Laramie County believe this is detrimental to a free market economy, and 
WHEREAS, the municipalities of Laramie County have recognized the necessity of local 
control regarding liquor licenses as it is the municipality who administers the emergency services 
and law enforcement, and 
WHEREAS, the municipalities of Laramie County have recognized the nationwide trend 
that most new liquor establishments are locating to former industrial, historical, and warehouse 
districts in dilapidated areas, revitalizing many blighted areas throughout the municipalities, and 
WHEREAS, the municipalities of Laramie County have requested the Wyoming 
Association of Municipalities to draft a similar resolution on this topic to address but not limited 
to, licenses regarding grocery store / convenience store sales, entertainment venues, gaming 
facilities, and removal of the census requirements, and 
WHEREAS, the municipalities of Laramie County request that upon passage of the 
modifications to State Statutes that they become effective immediately and not July 1, 2023. 
By Stefanie Boster at 11:37 am, Apr 22, 2022

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODIES OF 
LARAMIE COUNTY, WYOMING, that the municipalities of Laramie County hereby request the 
state legislative committees and state legislature review, consider, and adopt modifications to 
current State Statutes regarding the issuance of liquor licenses in the municipalities of all Wyoming 
communities.

Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282

MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Mark Christensen, Planner II 
Subject: A Resolution Approving an amendment to the Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan and the 
Hitching Post Urban Renewal Project Pursuant to Wyoming Statute § 15-9-110. 
Date: 
April 20, 2022 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is a Resolution.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Public Hearing  
04/25/2022 
• 
Public Services Committee  
 
05/03/2022 
• 
Second and Final Reading  
 
05/09/2022 
 
Summary:  
 
The Governing Body adopted the Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan on August 23, 2021. The approval of 
the plan permitted the use of tax increment financing (TIF) to aid the project in redevelopment. Eligible 
activities permitted under the Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan included the improvement of streets 
and sidewalks, the relocation of utilities, and the remediation of environmental contamination. On 
February 15, 2022, staff received a request to amend the Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan to include 
drainage and detention facilities as a public improvement eligible for URA funding. The amendments 
before you contain the inclusion of drainage and detention facilities as eligible Urban Renewal activities 
and updates the projected TIF amount to $3.7 million. The increase in the projected TIF is largely due to 
the addition of a second hotel.  
 
On March 3, 2022, the City Urban Renewal Authority authorized submission of the amendment to the 
Planning Commission for review and recommendation to the Governing Body. The Planning Commission 
met on April 4, 2022, and recommended approval of the Plan and Project area unanimously. The 
Planning Commission staff report is attached to this submittal and is unchanged. Though not required by 
State Statute, notice of the Public Hearing was sent to all property owners within the Project Area on 
April 19, 2022.  
 
Attachments: 
1. Resolution 
2. Planning Commission Staff Report 
3. Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

1 
 
RESOLUTION NO. ___________ 
 
ENTITLED:  “A RESOLUTION APPROVING AN AMENDMENT TO THE HITCHING 
POST URBAN RENEWAL PLAN AND THE HITCHING POST URBAN 
RENEWAL PROJECT PURSUANT TO WYOMING STATUTE § 15-9-110.” 
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to Resolution No. 6156 and under Wyoming Statute § 15-9-101, et 
seq., the City of Cheyenne found certain areas to be appropriate for an urban renewal project, 
including the “Hitching Post Study Area” as described in Resolution 6156; and 
 
WHEREAS, Resolution No. 6156 was adopted after hearings on June 14, 2021 and June 
28, 2021 and in conformity with Wyoming Statute § 15-9-106; and  
 
WHEREAS, Resolution No. 6167 was adopted on August 23, 2021 and approved the 
Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan and the Hitching Post Urban Renewal Project pursuant to 
Wyoming Statute § 15-9-110; and 
 
WHEREAS, Planning and Development staff received a request to amend the Hitching 
Post Urban Renewal Plan to include a provision allowing drainage and detention facilities as a 
public improvement eligible for tax increment financing funding on February 15, 2022; and  
 
WHEREAS, Planning and Development staff presented the proposed amendment to the 
Cheyenne Urban Renewal Authority Board on March 3, 2022; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne Urban Renewal Authority Board recommended unanimously 
that the provision allowing drainage and detention facilities be incorporated into the Hitching Post 
Urban Renewal Plan; and 
 
WHEREAS, Wyoming Statute § 15-9-108 requires that before approving an urban renewal 
project, the Governing Body shall have submitted the applicable urban renewal plan to the City 
Planning Commission, for review and recommendations as to its conformity with PlanCheyenne, 
which is the general plan for the development of the City of Cheyenne; and 
 
WHEREAS, on April 4, 2022, the City Planning Commission: (i) reviewed the Hitching 
Post Urban Renewal Plan for the Hitching Post Urban Renewal Project, (ii) found the Hitching 
Post Urban Renewal Plan to be in conformity with PlanCheyenne, and (iii) recommended that the 
Governing Body adopt the Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan for the Hitching Post Urban 
Renewal Project; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body held a public hearing on the Hitching Post Urban 
Renewal Plan and Project in accordance with Wyoming Statute § 15-9-109(a); and 
 
WHEREAS, on April 19, 2022, notice of the public hearing required by Wyoming Statute 
§ 15-9-109(a) was mailed to all property owners; and 
 
By Stefanie Boster at 8:32 am, Apr 19, 2022

 
2 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body has reviewed the Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan and 
Hitching Post Urban Renewal Project, and, for the reasons set forth in such plan is able to make, 
and hereby makes, the following findings: 
 
(i)  
The Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan conforms to PlanCheyenne, and is 
consistent with definite local objectives respecting appropriate land uses, improved 
traffic, public transportation, public utilities, recreational and community facilities 
and other public improvements; and 
  
(ii)   
The Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan is sufficiently complete to indicate land 
acquisition, demolition and removal of structures, redevelopment, improvements, 
and rehabilitation as may be proposed to be carried out in the urban renewal area, 
zoning and planning changes, if any, land used, maximum densities and building 
requirements; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body has reviewed the Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan and 
Hitching Post Urban Renewal Project, and, for the reasons set forth in such plan is able to make, 
and hereby makes, the following findings: 
 
(i)   
Because the Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan and project area boundaries do not 
encompass any residential properties, there is no need to designate a method for 
relocation of families displaced from the urban renewal area; 
  
(ii)   
The Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan conforms to PlanCheyenne as a whole; 
  
(iii)   The Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan gives due consideration to the provision of 
adequate park and recreational areas and facilities that may be desirable for 
neighborhood improvement, with special consideration for the health, safety and 
welfare of children residing in the general vicinity of the site covered by the 
Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan; 
  
(iv)   
The Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan affords maximum opportunity, consistent 
with the municipality's needs, for the rehabilitation or redevelopment of the urban 
renewal area by private enterprise; and 
 
WHEREAS approval of the Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan and Project on August 
23, 2021 authorized division of property taxes pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-120; and 
 
WHEREAS, division of property taxes shall be collected and deposited the Hitching 
Post Urban Renewal Authority Special Revenue Fund and segregated for the purpose of the 
Urban Renewal Authority; 
 
 
 
 

 
3 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, 
hereby approves the amendment to the Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan and Hitching Post 
Urban Renewal Project.

Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
PLANNING COMMISSION  
APRIL 4, 2022 
STAFF REPORT  
 
REQUEST: 
 
Review of and recommendation to the Governing Body of the Hitching Post Urban 
Renewal Plan and Project  
CASE NUMBER:  
URA-21-00001-01, Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan and Project Amendment 
PREPARED BY: 
Mark Christensen, AICP, Planner II 
 
 
 
 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION:  
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body amend the Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan and Project, as 
recommended by the Urban Renewal Authority, noting the request meets the review criteria of Wyo. 
Stat. § 15-9-110.  
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

URA-21-00001-01 
 
 
April 4, 2022 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan and Project was adopted by the City of Cheyenne Governing Body 
on August 23, 2021. The approval of the plan permitted the use of tax increment financing (TIF) to aid 
the project in redevelopment. Eligible activities permitted under the Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan 
included the improvement of streets and sidewalks, the relocation of utilities, and the remediation of 
environmental contamination. Under the previously adopted Plan the Urban Renewal Authority has 
received 4 applications for tax increment financing, with one application being brought through to a 
development agreement. Thus far the City and Urban Renewal Authority has allocated $1,200,000 
million in TIF funding and has active applications for an additional $1,033,000 for a total projected TIF 
obligation of $2,233,000.  
 
One of the previously mentioned applications had a request to utilize the TIF to fund stormwater 
drainage and detention facilities. Staff believes the construction of stormwater drainage and detention 
facilities are authorized activities under Wyoming State Statutes § 15-9-113; however, the Hitching Post 
Urban Renewal Plan and Project does not mention these improvements as eligible expenses. The Plan 
currently states, “the City of Cheyenne will use the Urban Renewal powers authorized to improve 
streets and sidewalks, relocate utilities, and remediate environmental contamination.” With this, staff 
felt the applicant should request that the URA Board consider whether stormwater drainage and 
detention facilities should be eligible for tax increment financing. The applicant provided the letter in 
attachment A to the Urban Renewal Authority Board for the regular meeting on March 3. The URA 
Board heard the item and recommended staff incorporate the proposed amendment into the Hitching 
Post Urban Renewal Plan and Project, moving the plan forward to the Planning Commission with a 
recommendation of approval. The amended language states: 
 
The City of Cheyenne will use the Urban Renewal powers authorized to improve streets and 
sidewalks, relocate utilities, construct other public improvements including stormwater drainage 
and detention facilities, and remediate environmental contamination, all of which are enabled by 
the Wyoming Urban Renewal Code – Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan, Page 5 
 
In addition to the amendment authorizing stormwater drainage and detention facilities as authorized 
activities under the Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan, the proposed amendment also updates the 
current tax increment financing projections included in the plan. Based on information provided by the 
applicant, the TIF projection has increased to $3.7 million. This is an increase from the previously utilized 
$2.7 million. The increase in the projection is due to the applicant providing more detail on the 
proposed redevelopment of the site, specifically the inclusion of a second hotel in Hitching Post Plaza. 
The TIF projection is not required to be included in the Plan by Wyoming State Statutes but does provide 
staff a basis when considering TIF applications. The updated projection is included in the Plan on page 6. 
 

URA-21-00001-01 
 
 
April 4, 2022 
 
REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
The Planning Commission’s role in adoption of the Urban Renewal Plan and Project is to review and 
provide a recommendation to the Governing Body as to whether or not to approve. The specific review 
criteria is listed in Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-110 and is summarized on pages seven and eight of the attached 
Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan and Project document.        
 
 
CONCLUSION:  
 
Staff concludes that this text amendment meets the applicable review criteria which is outlined in the 
attached Urban Renewal Plan and Project document.   
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Staff recommends the Planning Commission recommend approval of the Hitching Post Urban Renewal 
Plan and Project amendment as recommended by the Urban Renewal Authority, noting the request 
meets the review criteria of Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-110.  
 
 
ATTACHMENT: 
 
A. Applicant Request 
B. Hitching Post Plan and Project  
 
 

1102 WEST 19TH STREET  ●  P.O. BOX 301  ●  CHEYENNE, WYOMING  82003  ●  307/996-8281 
 
 
 
 
TIF FUNDING APPLICATION NARRATIVE 
HITCHING POST PLAZA 
 
Applicant: 
1600 Hitching Post, LLC 
Representative: 
Palma Land Planning, LLC / Lovas Engineering, P.C. 
Date: 
02/15/22 
 
 
 
Mr. Christensen, 
Per our discussions and our meeting with the City of Cheyenne Engineering Department and Floodplain 
Administrator, and due to the complex and various issues surrounding stormwater drainage and 
detention in and around the URA, we believe it is in the best interest of the City generally, and to the 
URA specifically, to expand the URA scope definitions of eligible activities to include drainage and 
detention facilities as a public improvement eligible for URA funding. 
If this type of public improvement is included, we believe that significant improvements can be made 
in the Crow Creek Drainage and collaboration between URA owners can be facilitated to reduce 
outflows from the area and improve the overall stormwater outflow volumes and water quality. 
As discussed in our meeting today, the most pertinent public improvements, as we see it, could include: 
• 
Replacement and re-alignment of ±575 LF of existing 30”/36” storm sewer main(s) currently 
outflowing into the Ice & Events Center regional detention facility, 
• 
Enlargement and reshaping of poorly functioning Ice & Events Center regional detention facility. 
• 
Potential enlargement and reshaping of poorly functioning “Townplace Suites” regional 
detention facility (upstream of Hitching Post property, 
• 
Excavation/Construction of stormwater detention facility(ies) to capture both existing and 
future surface flows. 
Please let me know if you and the other stakeholders and agencies believe this is in the best interest of 
the URA and meets the statutory and municipal requirements for activities which can be included; or if 
you have any questions or require additional information from me or the project engineer as it relates to 
this request. 
Thank you and best regards, 
 
 
Casey L. Palma, AICP 
Principal  |  Palma Land Planning 
Mobile: (307) 631-4776  |  Casey@PlanDesignWY.com 
www.PlanDesignWY.com
