of time not less than five (5) years following the last date that Consultant has 
provided services under this Agreement.  
 
 
Additional Insurance Information 
 
The Consultant shall name the City of Cheyenne as an Additional Insured by endorsement on its 
insurance policies and shall provide the City with a copy of the endorsements.  This requirement 
does not apply to workers’ compensation and professional liability insurance policies. 
 
Consultant shall provide the City with certificates of insurance acknowledging the above-stated 
coverages prior to beginning any work under this Agreement. 
 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory. All policies required 
under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the Agreement.  It shall be an affirmative 
obligation upon Consultant to immediately notify in writing the city risk manager, city clerk, and 
city attorney of any fact, circumstance, or occurrence that has resulted in or may result in the 
cancellation of, or substantive change to any insurance coverage required by this Agreement, and 
failure to do so shall be construed to be a breach of this Agreement. 
 
If requested by the City, the Consultant shall provide the City with copies of insurance policies 
and/or policy endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional insured.  The City’s 
failure to request or review such policies, endorsements, or certificates shall not affect the City’s 
rights or Consultant’s obligation hereunder. 
 
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a minimum A. M. 
Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
8. 
MINORITY AND DISADVANTAGED BUSINESS ENTERPRISES 49 C.F.R. Part 26 
All parties to this Agreement assure that no person will be excluded from participation in, denied 
the benefits of, or otherwise discriminated against, in connection with the award and performance 
of this Agreement on the grounds of age, race, color, disability, national origin or sex. 
 
9. 
COMPENSATION 
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the Consultant will bill 
the City and the City agrees to pay Consultant a not to exceed amount of One-hundred eighty 
thousand seven hundred dollars ($1805,700) stated in Exhibit A and Exhibit B.

10. 
MONTHLY REPORT 
With every monthly billing, Consultant shall provide the City Representative with a written 
statement of the status of the work with respect to the Scope of Work, time sheets, and work 
schedule. Failure to provide the required monthly report will delay processing of any payment 
request until the report is submitted. 
 
11. 
INDEPENDENT CONSULTANT 
The Consultant shall function as an independent consultant for the purposes of this Agreement. The 
Consultant shall assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities that may be incurred by the 
Consultant in fulfilling the terms of this Agreement. Nothing in this Agreement shall be interpreted 
as authorizing the Consultant or its agents or employees to act as an agent or representative of or on 
behalf of the City or to incur any obligation of any kind on the behalf of the City. 
 
12. 
TAXES 
The Consultant agrees to pay all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, debts and 
obligations incurred by and in connection with its operations under this Agreement. 
 
13. 
DEFAULT 
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material element of 
the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform according to the terms of 
this Agreement, such party may be declared in default. 
 
14. 
REMEDIES 
In the event a party has been declared in default, such defaulting party shall be allowed a period of 
fifteen (15) days within which to cure the default.  In the event the default remains uncorrected, the 
non-defaulting party declaring default may elect to: 
 
 
a. 
Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not exceed the 
contract amount; or 
 
 
b. 
Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance. 
 
15. 
TERM AND TERMINATION 
This Agreement shall end on or before June 30, 2023.  The parties may by mutual written 
agreement renew or extend this Agreement. 
The City may, without cause, and upon thirty days’ written notice to the Consultant, terminate this 
Agreement in whole or in part at any time, for the City’s convenience. Upon receipt of such notice, 
the Consultant shall: 
 
 
a. 
Discontinue all services affected; and 
 
 
b. 
Deliver to the City within five (5) days all data, drawings, specifications, reports, 
estimates, summaries, and such other information and materials as may have been 
accumulated by the Consultant in performing this Agreement, whether completed or 
in process. 
 
 
c. 
In the event of termination for convenience, the City will pay the Consultant for 
accepted work done and expenses incurred to the date of termination. Such 
acceptance shall not be unreasonably withheld.

of venue, choice of law, enforcement actions, and similar provisions should not be constructed as a

be strictly construed, either against or for either party, except that any ambiguity as to governmental 
or sovereign immunity shall be construed in favor of governmental immunity.

papers, and records pertinent to this Agreement; and to observe personnel in every phase of

20.

SUPPLEMENTAL CONDITIONS TO THE GENERAL CONDITIONS 
 
A. GENERAL CONDITIONS 
 
Standard of Care.  The standard of care for all professional engineering and related services 
performed or furnished by Engineer under this Agreement will be the care and skill ordinarily 
used by members of the subject profession practicing under similar circumstances at the same time 
and in the same locality.  The Engineer makes no warranties, express or implied, under this 
Agreement or otherwise, in connection with any services performed or furnished by Engineer.  
 
Technical Accuracy.  Owner shall not be responsible for discovering deficiencies in the 
technical accuracy of Engineer’s services.  Engineer shall correct deficiencies in technical 
accuracy without additional compensation unless such corrective action is directly attributable to 
deficiencies in Owner-furnished information.   
Conflicts within the Agreement.  If there is a conflict within this Agreement, the order of 
priority of the documents which make up this Agreement, from highest to lowest, will be: 
(a) Agreement. 
(c) Any supplementary conditions to the General Conditions. 
(d) General Conditions. 
(e) Schedule A – SCOPE OF SERVICES. 
(f) Other schedules to this Agreement. 
 
Any changes in the Consultant’s or Subconsultant’s staff or fee structure will be presented in writing 
to the City for approval prior to initiating any changes or performing any work. 
 
Overhead and Normal Business Expenses.  The consultant shall include customary overhead 
costs and statutory benefits as part including, but not limited to, social security contributions, 
unemployment, excise and payroll taxes, workers’ compensation, health and retirement benefits, 
bonuses, sick leave, vacation, and holiday pay, as applicable, within the billing rates furnished in 
Schedule B.  Additionally, the cost of general and administrative overhead which includes 
salaries and wages of employees engaged in business operations not directly chargeable to 
projects, plus non-Project operating costs, including but not limited to, business taxes, legal, rent, 
utilities, office supplies, insurance, and other operating costs shall not be directly billed to project 
and assumed to be included within the billing rates furnished.

with the project steering committee meetings.   The purpose of the log is to document and track 
major decisions during the design and development process. The log shall generally follow the 
format outlined below in Table 1 Design and Design Log:

PROPOSED AGREEMENT FOR SERVICES 
REVISED 
December 2021 
January 13, 2022 
Revised February 21, 2022 
Domenic Bravo 
Visit Cheyenne – Laramie County Convention & Visitors Bureau 
121 West 15th Street, Suite 202 
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
Re: 
Belvoir Ranch Pedestrian Bridge – Civil and Bridge Design Services 
Proposed Agreement for Civil Engineering Professional Services 
Mr. Bravo: 
Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. is pleased to submit our Proposed Agreement for Professional Civil 
Engineering Services to Visit Cheyenne – Laramie County Convention & Visitors Bureau in connection with 
the Belvoir Ranch Pedestrian Bridge – Civil and Bridge Design Services Project, located in Cheyenne, 
Wyoming. Our understanding of the Project scope and requirements is based upon an email and concept 
drawings received from your office, dated December 13, 2021, and a meeting/conversation with you on 
December 16, 2021. 
I. 
PROJECT DESCRIPTION 
The following constitutes our understanding of the Project and is the basis for our scope of services 
and the proposed fee. Significant changes to size and character of the Project may result in 
modifications to Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc.’s services and fees. 
A. The project consists of a new pedestrian bridge over the Union Pacific Railroad (UPRR) with the 
purpose to connect existing trails and recreation areas currently owned by the City of Cheyenne. 
The proposed bridge is to be located in Laramie County and east of railroad mile post 539.41 on 
property owned by the City of Cheyenne. The railroad right of way in this location is 
approximately 400 feet in total width, and all components of the bridge will be in the Union 
Pacific Right of way. The bridge components include gravel pedestrian and bike paths from 
existing trails located within 300 feet of the crossing, elevated approaches on either side of the 
bridge, and bridge abutments. The bridge structure is anticipated to be an approximately  
120 foot single-span, single-unit prefabricated steel truss superstructure, which will be 
developed as a performance specification by Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc., and delivered as a 
deferred submittal. Bridge substructures and contract requirements will be designed and 
developed by Martin/Martin, Wyoming, Inc. (referred to herein as the “Project”). 
EXHIBIT A  
(12 Pages)

Revised February 21, 2022

B. The following clarifications/understandings are included when defining 
Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc.’s Project scope of services:  
1. The City of Cheyenne is the “Owner” and will review documents as such. Laramie County is 
the AHJ and Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. intends to submit only to Laramie County for 
project Site Plan approval.  
2. Coordination with utilities, including water, sewer, communications, natural gas, electrical, 
site lighting, and bridge lighting and is excluded. 
3. Unless specified in “SERVICES” Railroad permit, review, or design fees required are excluded 
and are anticipated to be paid for by the Owner. 
4. Access agreements for any construction of future maintenance activities are excluded from 
this proposal. 
5. The design of water, sanitary, or storm sewer utilities or infrastructure is not anticipated and 
is excluded. 
6. Total project disturbance area will be less than 1 acre and the project will qualify for a 
Laramie County low-impact grading and erosion control permit. 
7. The City of Cheyenne will provide all property and title documentation. 
8. Existing drainage patterns around the crossing are not expected to change and a detailed 
drainage study or report is not included. 
9. Private utility locates or potholing services have been excluded. 
10. The proposed bridge is understood to be a prefabricated bridge, designed by the bridge 
supplier.  
C. The Project will be completed with a single integrated design and construction package. 
D. The design of the Project will take into appropriate consideration the applicable regulations of 
the following Authorities Having Jurisdiction (AHJ). The design may not comply with documents, 
requirements, codes other than those required by the AHJ and as listed below, unless the Client 
specifically directs, and Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. agrees to conformance to additional codes 
and references during the design period: 
1. City or County Standards and Regulations: 
a. Laramie County 
b. WYDOT Standard Specifications for Road and Bridge Construction 
c. WYDOT Bridge Design Manual 
d. AASHTO LRFD Bridge Design Specifications 
e. AASHTO LRFD Guide Specifications for the Design of Pedestrian Bridges 
f. American Railway Engineering and Maintenance-of-Way Association (AREMA) Manual 
for Railway Engineering 
2. Special District Regulations: 
a. Union Pacific Railroad

II. 
DEFINITIONS 
A. Scope of Site Design: Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc.’s Proposed Agreement includes the services 
listed herein only to extents required for construction and excavation related to these bridge 
and site elements. No design of elements beyond the project limits is included unless specifically 
identified herein. 
B. Perimeter Drain: Foundation drain placed near the base of the bridge abutments, wingwalls, or 
retaining walls. 
C. Deferred Submittals: Elements or components of the Project which are performance specified 
(as a deferred submittal in accordance with the International Building Code), but not designed 
by the Engineer-of-Record. Deferred design elements are designed by or under the supervision 
of a Professional Engineer, registered in the state where the Project is located, and retained by 
the material or component supplier. 
D. Assumed Conditions: Where existing conditions cannot be verified prior to or during design due 
to lack of access, Project schedule, or economic constraints, the Contract Documents will be 
developed based on available information or assumed conditions. Assumed conditions will be 
identified on the drawings and verified by the Contractor during the construction phase of the 
Project. Existing condition variations from assumed conditions will be evaluated by 
Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. and may result in Contract Document changes. Such changes may 
increase the construction schedule and result in additional design and construction costs. 
III. 
SERVICES 
Services will include submittals of required civil engineering plans and reports to the AHJ and the 
Owner (City of Cheyenne) for review in support of a site plan submittal, jurisdictional submittal and 
associated civil engineering design documents (as listed herein). Unless stipulated otherwise in this 
agreement, preparation of site plan documents (outside those listed herein) and processing of the 
submittal(s) will be by others.  
A. BASIC SERVICES 
1. Survey 
a. A topographic and boundary survey, including location of site features horizontally, 
elevations at 1-foot contours, visible utilities, roadways, and the railway. Critical items 
including the railroad and marked utilities will be collected utilizing conventional survey 
methods where the balance of the topographic survey will be via drone. Utility locates 
are included as identified below. Railroad roadway worker protection, including railroad 
flagging/flagmen, and all other on-track safety requirements will be provided by Union 
Pacific Railroad. Survey will be AutoCAD format. An ALTA survey per ACSM Standards is 
not included. 
b. Utility Locates: Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. will contact Wyoming 811 locate service to 
field locate utilities. UPRR will provide field locates of private utilities on UPRR  

Belvoir Ranch Pedestrian Bridge – Civil and Bridge Design Services 
January 13, 2022 
Revised February 21, 2022 
P a g e 4 | 12 
right-of-way. The field located existing utilities will be shown on the survey and it shall 
be the Contractor’s responsibility to field locate and verify size, material, and horizontal 
and vertical location of all utilities, including any not field located during the design, 
prior to construction. 
c. Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc., through a third-party surveyor Steil Surveying Services 
LLC, will prepare land descriptions (including a graphic representation of the land 
description) associated with the creation of a right-of-way easement specific to the 
bridge crossing and the approaches for the Union Pacific Railroad. Easements for any 
other entity is excluded. It is assumed that the creation of the required legal language, 
documentation, submittals/negotiations, environmental study (if required), title work, 
and recording of final agreements will be by others. 
2. Coordination with UPRR 
a. Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. through a third-party consultant, 360 Rail Services, will 
coordinate with the Union Pacific Railroad during design. Coordination includes initial 
contact with the Railroad to establish railroad design requirements and determining 
their engineering review fees. All review fees and permit costs are excluded from this 
proposal. Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. through a third-party consultant, will be 
responsible for the review and compilation of design submittals to the Railroad. 
Included is one (1) site visit during design. 
b. Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. has included up to three (3) days of railroad safety for site 
visits by Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. and its Subconsultants. Martin/Martin Wyoming, 
Inc. and its Sub-Consultants will carry individual Railroad Protective Liability Insurance 
policies.  
3. Non-Railroad Entitlements 
a. Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. will act as the primary point of contact and submittal 
champion for Site Plan submittals to the AHJ for review and approval and 
complimentary copies to the owner (City of Cheyenne). Processing includes providing 
the title block, viewport, and scale for distribution to the Design Team, submitting the 
collected package to the local jurisdiction, coordinating with the Design Team for 
comment assignments, and compiling comment responses. Martin/Martin  
Wyoming, Inc. will be responsible for creation of the cover sheet, existing conditions 
survey plan, site plan, and grading/utility plan, but will require input from other Design 
Team members for the content of the cover sheet and site plan. Furthermore, 
development of the following Site Development Plan sheets are excluded: landscape 
plan, architectural elevations, photometric plan, and details sheets. Processing of an 
Urban Design, Design Review or Material Boards, Architectural Control Committees, 
Landmark, or Historical District submittals is excluded. Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. will 
work in a timely fashion to respond to AHJ Site Plan comments; however, it does not 
have authority over the timeliness of AHJ review, nor final approval. 
4. Civil and Bridge Engineering 
a. One (1) site visit is anticipated during the design phase of the project.

b. A detailed grading and drainage plan will be prepared for the Project site, including 
1-foot contours and spot elevations around the buildings and in the parking lots. The 
grading and drainage plans will be based on criteria (including geotechnical report) 
established and provided by the Client.  
c. A site drainage analysis will be performed to demonstrate that the proposed project will 
not significantly impact existing drainage patterns. This will include a narrative and 
letter outlining the findings and justification to be provided to the AHJ and the owner 
(City of Cheyenne) and the Railroad. If a detailed drainage study and report is required, 
a separate proposal will be provided for additional work.  
d. A low-impact grading and erosion control plan (GESC) permit will be prepared for the 
Project site construction activities, as the disturbed area is anticipated to be less than 1 
acre and the project will not impact downstream receiving waters.  
e. A site layout plan showing the horizontal location of proposed paths and bridge 
components. Also shown on this plan will be proposed types of pavement and 
thicknesses of each, as indicated in the soils report supplied by the Client. The initial site 
plan layout will be furnished by the Architect in CAD. 
f. Site construction phasing plans to include anticipated sequencing of major components 
will be provided at the preliminary and final design stages. 
g. Preparing engineer’s opinion of probable cost estimates for the Project will be provided 
at the Concept, Preliminary, and Final Stages submittals. Any opinions of probable 
Project costs or construction costs rendered by Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. represent 
its reasonable professional engineering opinion and are furnished for general guidance. 
Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. makes no representation, warranty, or guarantee, either 
expressed or implied, as to the accuracy of such opinions as compared to bid or actual 
costs. 
h. Up to Twenty-Five (25) bridge construction plans to include an anticipated list of

12) Deck Slab and Section (1)

16) Railing Details (1)

i.

improvements. 
j. 
Included in this scope of work are an estimated six (6) design coordination meetings of

“ADDITIONAL SERVICE” and billed hourly. 
k. Services during the bidding phase by answering the Contractor’s questions, as

l. 
Construction Administration services, including review of material submittals, answering

B. OPTIONAL SERVICES 
These services are not included in our base fee but can be provided upon request and written 
authorization: 
1. Option I – Record Drawings: Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. will provide “Record Drawings” 
(based upon information provided to Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. by the Project 
Contractor(s)) in electronic and drafted format. A field survey of as-built conditions certified 
by a Professional Land Surveyor is not currently included. 
C. ADDITIONAL SERVICES 
Any services not listed under “BASIC SERVICES” or selected/authorized “OPTIONAL SERVICES” 
above, are considered “ADDITIONAL SERVICES” whether specifically listed below or not. The 
compensation for these services will be in addition to that for “BASIC SERVICES” and will be 
billed at the hourly rates listed under “COMPENSATION” or as a mutually negotiated lump sum. 
If during the progress of work, Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. determines “ADDITIONAL 
SERVICES” are recommended or requested, these “ADDITIONAL SERVICES” will be negotiated 
directly with the Client. “ADDITIONAL SERVICES” include, but are not limited to the following: 
1. This proposal anticipates the fees necessary for the initial meetings, design and preparation, 
and submittal of the application package. Upon receipt of the comments from the review 
agencies, Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. will review them with the Client and determine if 
any additional fees are necessary to address comments that are either unwarranted, could 
not be reasonably anticipated prior to submittal, or are beyond the scope of this proposal. 
The Client will be asked to approve these additional fees prior to Martin/Martin Wyoming, 
Inc. commencing work. The same process may be necessary upon receipt of subsequent 
comments. Any comments that are generated by the AHJ or owner (City of Cheyenne), 
which are a result of Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc.’s negligence, or simply a reasonable 
clarification of our design intents, will not be included in the request for additional fees.

Revised February 21, 2022 
P a g e 7 | 12 
2. Investigating or surveying existing conditions or facilities, or making measured drawings 
thereof, or verifying the accuracy of information furnished by others. 
3. Preparing documents for early or alternate bid packages, out-of-sequence services, or 
phased construction. 
4. Providing digital data other than those indicated in the “DELIVERABLES” Section of this 
Agreement. 
5. Traffic analysis, including access review and requirements for accel/decel lanes, if required. 
6. Preparing “as-built” survey or record drawings (unless executed as an optional or base 
service). 
7. Providing services in connection with future facilities, systems, or equipment, which are not 
intended to be constructed as part of this Project except as noted under “BASIC SERVICES.” 
8. Attending Owner/Architect/Contractor meetings during construction, except as noted under 
“BASIC SERVICES.” 
9. BIM/3D coordination or modeling beyond what is required for producing two-dimensional 
Construction Documents. “ADDITIONAL SERVICES” will be based on a BIM execution plan 
indicating the level of modeling required and intended use of the model. 
10. Services during construction except as noted under “BASIC SERVICES.” 
11. Preparing AHJ required construction certifications (including but not limited to grading 
certifications, utility certifications, etc.) and or certifying the constructed facilities meet the 
intent of the construction plans (unless this scope is provided and included specifically as a 
base scope of service or as an executed and authorized optional scope of services, provided 
in writing). 
12. Design of off-site improvements not listed under “BASIC SERVICES,” such as lane widening to 
adjacent streets or extending utility or drainage mains to the site. 
13. FEMA map revisions. 
14. Cathodic Protection Designs. 
15. Land subdivision-platting services. 
16. Retaining walls not attached to the bridge abutment or wingwalls. 
17. Structural design of site elements including sign bases, light pole bases, etc. 
18. Detailed layout data for landscape features. 
19. Coordination of regulated utilities. 
20. LEED™ submittal documents (unless executed as an optional or base service).  
21. Perimeter Drain and Underdrain Layout Drawings (unless included and executed as an 
optional service up above). 
a. Subject to provisions 1) – 4) below, provide layout plans for perimeter drains and 
underdrains for the proposed building. The plan will indicate the size, horizontal and 
vertical position of the pipe, and location of cleanouts for use in layout and installation 
of the drain. Construction details of the drain and cleanouts are defined on documents 
produced by others. 
1) The following design parameters shall be provided to Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. 
in the Project’s geotechnical report: 
a) Anticipated groundwater flow rate into the system and drainage patterns 
around and below the building. 
b) Proposed underdrain sizes, materials, and minimum slopes required. 

Belvoir Ranch Pedestrian Bridge – Civil and Bridge Design Services 
January 13, 2022 
Revised February 21, 2022 
P a g e 8 | 12 
c) Construction details of the perimeter drain and underdrain as it relates to the 
proposed foundation system, including drainage, rock, piping, backfill, drainage 
boards, and geotextile fabric. Relationship to foundation with dimensions is also 
required. 
d) Recommendations for minimum and maximum depth dimension from finish 
floor to pipe invert. 
e) Recommendations for spacing and number of cleanouts required for the 
system. 
2) If a free drainage outfall for the underdrain system is not available, 
Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. will coordinate a location for sump pit. Design of the 
pump, electrical, and alarm system will be by others. Pressure outfall line to 
drainage system will be depicted on Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc.’s drainage plan. 
3) Written approval of the layout plans by the Geotechnical Engineer, identifying any 
items not in compliance with the recommendations of the geotechnical report for 
the Project shall be required prior to submittal of final stamped plans by 
Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. 
4) The following quality control activities for perimeter drain and underdrain 
installation, provided by others, will be included as Contract Document 
requirements: 
a) Full-time inspection by the Geotechnical Engineer to verify compliance with 
their design recommendations and the Contract Documents. 
b) A final survey stamped by the Contractor’s surveyor recording the as-built 
position of the underdrain system with vertical and horizontal control points at 
each cleanout and distances not exceeding 25 feet. 
c) Video inspection of the entire length of the system demonstrating free clear 
flow after building back fill for the Project is completed. 
D. CONTINGENT SERVICES 
“CONTINGENT SERVICES” are services that arise due to circumstances not known at the time of 
this proposed Agreement that could not be readily anticipated. The compensation for these 
services will be in addition to that for “BASIC SERVICES,” and will be billed at the hourly rates 
listed under “COMPENSATION.” If during the progress of work, Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. 
determines “CONTINGENT SERVICES” are recommended or requested, these “SERVICES” will be 
negotiated with the Client, or if directed, with the Owner, Developer, or Contractor. 
“CONTINGENT SERVICES” include, but are not limited to the following: 
1. Engineering services resulting from changes in the Project scope beyond Martin/Martin 
Wyoming, Inc.’s control. 
2. Services in connection with submitting work due to changes in government requirements 
instituted after the date of this Proposed Agreement. 
3. Making revisions to drawings, specifications, or other documents when such revisions are 
inconsistent with prior approvals or instructions. 

Belvoir Ranch Pedestrian Bridge – Civil and Bridge Design Services 
January 13, 2022 
Revised February 21, 2022 
P a g e 9 | 12 
4. Providing consultation and professional services in connection with the evaluation or 
replacement of any work damaged during construction. 
5. Services made necessary by the Contractor’s default or by defects in the work of the 
Contractor. 
6. Providing more extensive representation and/or observation at the work site during 
construction (at the request of the Owner, Contractor, or Architect), than listed under 
“BASIC SERVICES.” 
7. Post-construction services. 
8. Furnishing of opinions as an expert or serving as an expert witness. 
9. Services modifying work performed by Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. to bring the 
construction cost within any limitation imposed by the Client. 
IV. 
CLIENT PROVIDED INFORMATION 
Completion of the services identified herein are dependent on the Client providing the following 
information in a timely fashion that Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. may rely on: 
A. Furnish all soil analysis/reports (including, but not limited to pavement design, subsurface and 
environmental tests and reports) required for design of the Project. Informational needs and 
subsurface explorations will be provided in accordance with the AASHTO LRFD Bridge Design 
Specifications (9th Edition) Section 10 and the AREMA Manual for Railway Engineering Part 22. 
Subsurface exploration locations/data will be sufficient in number and depth to establish a 
reliable longitudinal and transverse substrata profile at areas of concern such as at structure 
foundation locations and adjacent earthwork locations, and to investigate and adjacent 
geological hazards that could affect the structure performance. The Client will require the 
Project Geotechnical Engineer to review the Design and Construction Documents (including 
drawings and specifications) prepared by Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. for conformance with 
the geotechnical recommendations and provide written comments prior to finalization of the 
Martin/Martin, Inc. plans. The Client also accepts that the Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. design 
services are based upon and relies upon the geotechnical findings and recommendations. 
B. Provide design criteria for the Project, including coordination of Building Code and ADA routes, 
ADA Compliance, and ADA reporting/certification as it relates to site design elements. 
C. All corridor aesthetics, environmental considerations, and architectural concepts, if applicable. 
D. When the Client selects Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc.’s scope and does not include services 
needed to attest to the final conditions required for certifications required by the AHJ, any 
certifications required by AHJ shall be prepared, processed and submitted by others. If the AHJ 
requires the Engineer-of-Record to provide certifications, the Client shall require the Project’s 
responsible/knowledgeable individuals to provide Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. written 
certification of construction’s suitability prior to Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. signing the AHJ 
certification. Client accepts and acknowledges that Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. is relying upon 
these individuals’ written representation and is not liable for the accuracy or suitability of the 

Belvoir Ranch Pedestrian Bridge – Civil and Bridge Design Services 
January 13, 2022 
Revised February 21, 2022 
P a g e 10 | 12 
elements that Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. either did not design or designed but did not 
observe the construction thereof. 
V. 
DELIVERABLES  
A. Survey: Topographic Design Survey. Boundary Survey. Survey will be in AutoCAD format. 
B. Concept Engineering Plan: including site plan, crossing profile, typical section of the crossing, 
preliminary phasing plan, and a photo log of the proposed project location. 
C. Preliminary Engineering Plan: includes project map, existing utilities, proposed horizontal layout 
and control, plan and profile of crossing and approaches, and construction phasing plan. 
D. Final Engineering and Construction Plans: includes project map, existing utilities, proposed 
horizontal layout and control, plan and profile of crossing and approaches, and construction 
phasing plan. 
E. Planning and Engineering Department Submittal: Includes final grading plans and final GESC 
plans and report. 
F. Electronic Terrain model produced in Civil 3D version 2022 subject to Martin/Martin Wyoming, 
Inc. Electronic Document Release Agreement (available upon request). The party that receives 
the electronic model takes all responsibility for derivative models and any activities and/or 
actions that may rely on any electronic data prepared or provided by Martin/Martin Wyoming, 
Inc. In the event of any conflict between the electronic model and paper documents, the paper 
documents govern.  
VI. 
PROJECT SCHEDULE 
A. Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. estimates a minimum of 28 working days to prepare data for 
initial conceptual submittals for review by the AHJ from the date of receipt of the signed 
Contract, and receipt/completion of design survey, and electronic site plan, and the 
geotechnical report. 
B. Subsequent design submittal milestones shall be reviewed and updated with “Client” after initial 
submittals comments are evaluated. 
VII. 
TERMS AND CONDITIONS OF SERVICE 
All work directed in writing by the Client and completed by Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. prior to 
the execution of the Agreement for the Project is subject to the Terms and Conditions noted in 
Exhibit A. The Terms and Conditions are incorporated into and become a part of this Proposed 
Agreement and form the basis of the scope of services and the proposed fee. Martin/Martin 
Wyoming, Inc. reserves the right to modify the fee in response to any changes to these Terms and 
Conditions in the final Agreement. 

Belvoir Ranch Pedestrian Bridge – Civil and Bridge Design Services 
January 13, 2022 
Revised February 21, 2022

VIII. COMPENSATION 
A. “BASIC SERVICES” will be provided for a lump sum fee as follows: 
Survey: .............................................................................................................................. $9,000 
Railroad Coordination: .................................................................................................. $17,000 
Concept Civil/Bridge Design: ......................................................................................... $26,800  
Preliminary Civil/Bridge Design: ................................................................................... $62,800  
Final Civil/Bridge Design: ............................................................................................... $30,000  
Bidding/Construction Services: ................................................. Hourly Estimated Fee $26,600  
 
TOTAL: $172,200 
B. Non-labor expense, including printing, plotting, deliveries, transportation, subsistence, lodging, 
and mileage is reimbursable at the rates listed below in addition to the fee stated and is 
estimated to be $5,000. 
C. “ADDITIONAL SERVICES” and “CONTINGENT SERVICES” will be provided on an hourly basis 
following the “HOURLY RATES” listed below. 
D. HOURLY RATES: 
Principal .................................... $195 per hour Engineer EIT I ................................ $95 per hour 
Associate.................................... $165 per hour Sr. Designer ................................. $130 per hour 
Sr. Project Engineer ................... $155 per hour Designer ...................................... $115 per hour 
Sr. Bldg. Envelope Specialist ...... $155 per hour Sr. Construction Services Rep ..... $135 per hour 
Project Engineer ........................ $135 per hour Technician III ............................... $100 per hour 
GPR Certified Invest Engr .......... $135 per hour Technician II .................................. $90 per hour 
Bldg. Envelope Specialist ........... $135 per hour Technician I ................................... $80 per hour 
Professional Engineer ................ $120 per hour Administrative Assistant ............... $75 per hour 
Engineer EIT II ............................ $105 per hour Engineering Intern ........................ $75 per hour 
Rates for personnel above are subject to change each year. 
E. It is assumed that Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. will not need to provide design and construction 
administration services related to this proposal beyond December 2024. If Martin/Martin 
Wyoming, Inc. is required to provide services beyond this date under this Agreement, 
compensation will be increased 5% per six-month period beyond that date.

F. REIMBURSABLE EXPENSES 
1. Cost of (out-of-town) mileage for travel to and from Project meetings, the Project site, and 
other travel by automobile required to complete the services will be reimbursable at the 
current GSA Rates. 
2. Other consultant or professional services provided by Steil Surveying Services LLC and 
360 Rail Solutions, but furnished by Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. under this Agreement. 
3. Non-labor expense such as transportation, subsistence, lodging, field office, outside 
reproduction, and shipping/messenger service billed to Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. 
4. Funds advanced by Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. for permit fees, applications, and 
incidental Project fees will be reimbursed to Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. at Martin/Martin 
Wyoming, Inc.’s cost. 
IX. 
ACCEPTANCE AND AUTHORITY 
This Agreement, together with the Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. “Terms and Conditions of Service 
(Exhibit ‘A’),” and any other Attachments and Exhibits hereto and made a part hereof, represents the entire 
Agreement and supersedes all prior negotiations, representations, or agreements, whether written or oral. 
Amendment shall be by written instrument only, signed by all parties. 
Please sign below and return to Martin/Martin Wyoming, Inc. as authorization to proceed.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-04-25_TotalExtraction_79873002.pdf
MEMORANDUM OF UNDERSTANDING 
BETWEEN THE CITY OF CHEYENNE AND 
LARAMIE COUNTY SCHOOL DISTRICT NUMBER ONE 
 
1. Parties. The Memorandum of Understanding (“MOU”) is made and entered into by and 
between Laramie County School District Number One (“District”), whose address is 2810 
House Avenue, Cheyenne, WY 82001, and the City of Cheyenne, a municipality 
organized and existing under the laws of the state of Wyoming, acting through its 
Community Recreation and Events Division (“City”), whose address is 2101 O’Neil 
Avenue, Room 302, Cheyenne, WY 82001. 
 
2. Purpose. The purpose of the MOU is to provide Civic Center usage to Laramie County 
School District Number One Music Department. The city agrees to provide, up to three 
(3) free one day events not to exceed three thousand dollars ($3,000.00) per event.   Any 
cost over the three thousand dollars ($3,000.00) per event will be paid by the District. 
 
3. Terms. The MOU shall commence on March 1, 2022 and shall remain in full force 
and effect through March 31, 2027. 
 
4. Civic Center Use and Scheduling. The District, through the office of the Fine Arts 
Coordinator, will schedule the three music events with the civic center designee 
according to the scheduling and contract procedures put in place by the City. The 
District will abide by all Civic Center policies put in place at the time of use. The 
District will provide the City with a liability insurance certificate. 
 
5. General Provisions. 
 
A. Amendments. Either party may request changes in this MOU. Any changes, 
modifications, revisions, or amendments to this MOU which are mutually agreed 
upon by and between parties to this MOU shall be incorporated by written 
instrument, executed and signed by all parties to this MOU. 
 
B. Applicable Law. The construction, interpretation, and enforcement of this MOU 
shall be governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming. The courts of the State 
of Wyoming shall have jurisdiction over any action arising out of the MOU and 
over the parties, and the venue shall by First Judicial District, Laramie County, 
Wyoming. 
 
C. Assignments.  This MOU shall be binding upon and inure to the benefit of the 
parties hereto and their respective heirs, successors, transferees, and permitted 
assignees. Neither party shall assign this MOU without the express written 
consent of the other. 
By Stefanie Boster at 11:00 am, Apr 22, 2022

D. Availability of Funds. Each payment obligation of either party is conditioned 
upon the availability of government funds, which are appropriated or allocated for 
the payment of this obligation. If funds are not allocated and available for the 
continuance of the services performed by either party, either party may terminate 
the MOU at the end of the period for which the funds are available. Each party 
shall notify the other party at the earliest possible time of the services which will 
or may be affected by a shortage of funds. No penalty shall accrue to either party 
in the event this provision is exercised, and neither party shall be obligated or 
liable for any future payments due for any damages as a result of termination 
under this section. This provision shall not be construed to permit either party to 
terminate this MOU in order to acquire similar services from another party. 
 
E. Entirety of Agreement. This MOU, consisting of three (3) pages, represent the 
entire and integrated agreement between the parties and supersedes all prior 
negotiations, representations and agreements, whether written or oral. 
 
F. Force Majeure. The performance of the MOU by either party shall be subject to 
force majeure including, but not limited to, acts of God, fire, flood, natural 
disaster, war or threat of war, acts or threats of terrorism, civil disorder, 
unauthorized strikes, governmental regulation or advisory, recognized health 
threats as determined by the World Health Organization, the Centers for Disease 
Control, or local government authority or health agencies, curtailment of 
transportation facilities, or other similar occurrence or similar ones prevent, 
dissuade, or unreasonably delay the performance required by the MOU. The 
MOU may be cancelled by either party, without liability, damages, fees, or 
penalty, and any unused deposits or amounts paid shall be refunded, for any one 
or more of the above reasons, by written notice of the other party. 
 
G. Notices. All notices arising out of or from the provisions of this MOU shall be 
in writing and given to the parties either by regular mail or delivery in person. 
 
H. Prior Approval. This MOU shall not be binding upon either party unless this 
MOU has been reduced to writing before performance begins. 
 
I. Governmental Immunity. The City of Cheyenne and Laramie County School 
District Number One do not waive their governmental immunity by entering this 
MOU, and each fully retains all immunities and defenses provided by law with 
respect to any action based on or occurring as a result of this MOU. 
 
J. Indemnity. Each party to this agreement shall be responsible for any liability 
arising from its own conduct. Neither party agrees to insure, defend, or 
indemnify the other. 

K. Third Party Beneficiary Rights. The parties do not intend to create in any other 
individual or entity the status of third-party beneficiary, and the MOU shall not 
be construed so as to create such status. The rights, duties and obligations 
contained in this MOU shall operate only between the parties to the MOU and 
shall inure solely to the benefit of the parties to this MOU. The provisions of this 
MOU are intended only to assist the parties to this MOU intend and expressly 
agree that only parties’ signatory to this MOU shall have any legal or equitable 
right to seek to enforce this MOU, to seek any remedy arising out of a party’s 
performance or failure to perform any term or condition of this MOU, or to bring 
an action for the breach of the MOU. 
 
6. Signatures. In witness whereof, the parties to this MOU through their duly authorized 
representatives have executed the MOU on the days and dates set out below, and certify 
that they have read, understood, and agreed to the terms and conditions of the MOU as 
set forth herein.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-04-25_TotalExtraction_76915064.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Mark Christensen, Planner II 
Subject: Development Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Swagger Construction, LLC, and 
Banner Capital Bank for Tax Increment Financing for Hitching Post Plaza 
Date: 
April 20, 2022 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is a Development Agreement related to distribution of tax increment 
financing (TIF) revenue for overages related to remediation, demolition, and cleanup of environmental 
hazards at a site located at 1600 West Lincolnway and commonly known as the Hitching Post Inn. 
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Public Hearing  
04/25/2022 
• 
Public Services Committee  
 
05/03/2022 
• 
Second and Final Reading  
 
05/09/2022 
 
Summary:  
 
The attached Development Agreement is between the City of Cheyenne, Swagger Construction, LLC, and 
Banner Capital Bank for reimbursement of overage costs for environmental remediation and cleanup 
associated with the redevelopment of the Hitching Post property. Reimbursement will come from the 
TIF revenue generated by the property. The new approved TIF amount is not to exceed $1.66 million as 
the applicant is requesting an additional $460,000 in TIF assistance. Based on staff projections, the 
Hitching Post Plaza project is expected to generate a TIF amount between $2.7 and $3 million (and 
potentially up to $3.7 million) over a 25-year period. The TIF was enabled by the adoption of the 
Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan and Project on August 23, 2021. 
 
The Urban Renewal Authority (URA) approved Swagger Construction, LLC’s application for the additional 
TIF on April 7, 2022. City Council approval of the Development Agreement is required due to the 
Hitching Post Urban Renewal Authority special fund being held by the City. The terms of the agreement 
stipulate that the applicant will be reimbursed from the URA special fund as revenue is generated from 
the specific project and the applicant will be responsible for any costs beyond the amount of revenue 
generated should the TIF increment underperform. The agreement is currently under review by Banner 
Capital Bank and any changes on their behalf will be discussed at Public Services Committee on May 3, 
2022. 
 
Attachments: 
1. Development Agreement

DEVELOPMENT AGREEMENT 
THIS DEVELOPMENT AGREEMENT (this “Agreement”) is executed, delivered, and 
entered into this __ day of _____________ 2022, by and between the City of Cheyenne, a 
municipal corporation of the State of Wyoming (“City”), Banner Capital Bank (“Lender”), 
Swagger Construction, LLC (“Developer”), and 1600 Hitching Post, LLC (“Owner”). 
RECITALS: 
A. 
WHEREAS the property legally described as Lot 1, Block 1, P. A. S. Subdivision, 
First Filing (“Property”), owned in fee-simple by Owner, to be known as Hitching Post Plaza, is 
encumbered by unsafe, contaminated, and blighted structures which are a public health and safety 
hazard (“Structures”) as illustrated on Exhibit A attached hereto and incorporated herein; and 
B. 
WHEREAS Developer has granted a note to Lender and as further security 
Developer intends to grant Lender the right to receive any reimbursements that it may be entitled 
to from the City as contemplated in this Agreement until the balance of the loan is paid in full.   
C. 
WHEREAS Developer is engaged in the business of developing and redeveloping 
real estate, and in conjunction with the proposed “Hitching Post Plaza” project (“URA”), is 
contracted by Owner to demolish, abate, and remove those certain Structures in conformance with 
demolition permit(s) approved by the City Building Department, and 
D.
WHEREAS the Property is within an “urban renewal area” as defined by W.S. §
15-9-103 and as designated, approved, and adopted by the City as the Hitching Post Urban
Renewal Plan and Project Area pursuant to the Wyoming Urban Renewal Code, W.S. § 15-9-101,
et seq. (“URA Code”); and
E.
WHEREAS the URA Code authorizes the City to engage in various activities as
part of an “urban renewal project” as defined in W.S. § 15-9-103, which undertakings and activities 
may include: (i) acquisition of a slum area or a blighted area or portion thereof; (ii) demolition and 
removal of buildings and improvements; (iii) installation, construction, or reconstruction of streets, 
utilities, parks, playgrounds, and other improvements necessary for carrying out in the URA the 
urban renewal objectives of this Agreement in accordance with the Plan (defined below in 
Paragraph 4); (iv) disposition of any property acquired in the URA at its fair value for uses in 
accordance with the Plan; (v) carrying out plans for a program of voluntary or compulsory repair 
and rehabilitation of buildings or other improvements in accordance with the Plan; and (vi) 
acquisition of any other real property in the URA if necessary to eliminate unhealthy, unsanitary 
or unsafe conditions, lessen density, eliminate obsolete or other uses detrimental to the public 
welfare, or otherwise to remove or prevent the spread of blight or deterioration or to provide land 
for needed public facilities (collectively, the foregoing permitted urban renewal project 
undertakings and activities are defined as the “Statutorily Permitted URA Scope”); and 
F.
WHEREAS in relation to the Statutorily Permitted URA Scope, the City has been
granted certain powers pursuant to the URA Code, including, but not limited to, the powers listed 
in W.S. § 15-9-113 and -115 through -118 (the “Specifically Enumerated URA Powers”); and 
By Stefanie Boster at 8:20 am, Apr 20, 2022

2 
G.
WHEREAS Developer proposes to demolish, abate, and remove those certain
Structures as part of the implementation of the Plan in the URA, which the scope of work 
(including but not limited to labor, materials, equipment, transport, disposal fees, and site cleanup 
and site stabilization) is described and defined on Exhibit A attached hereto (the “Developer 
Work”); and 
H.
WHEREAS Developer Work is in the interest of the health, safety, and welfare of
the public and the reimbursement of the cost of the Developer Work and the performance of the 
City’s obligations under this Agreement are within the Statutorily Permitted URA Scope and the 
Specifically Enumerated URA Powers, the City has the intent and authority to reimburse the 
Lender for the Developer Work on the terms provided in this Agreement; and 
I.
WHEREAS the parties have agreed, in consideration of the foregoing statements,
to execute this Agreement subject to and on the terms and conditions set forth herein. 
NOW THEREFORE, in consideration of the mutual promises contained herein and for other 
good and valuable consideration, the receipt and adequacy of which are hereby acknowledged, 
the Parties agree as follows: 
Recitals Incorporated. The foregoing recitals are incorporated herein by reference into this 
Agreement as though set forth at length.  
List of Exhibits: 
A. Exhibit A - Structures/Phases/Property
B. Exhibit B - Developer Work and Estimated Costs
C. URA/TIF Term Sheet
D. Use of Funds
E. Overage Request
Terms: The Parties agree as follows: 
1.
Performance of Developer Work. Subject to the terms and conditions contained
herein, the Developer shall complete Developer Work on a phase-by-phase basis and as described 
in Exhibit B. 
2.
Approvals. It shall be the obligation of the Developer, its contractors, subcontractors,
employees, and its successors and/or assigns to obtain any local, county, state and federal 
governmental permits, and approvals necessary to undertake and perform Developer Work, as 
generally described on the Plan (as defined below in Paragraph 4) and otherwise; including, but not 
limited to, (i) an individual grading, demolition, building, planning or other permit for each Structure 
to be removed and for which such permit is required; (ii) the posting of a performance guaranty and 
inspection escrow associated with demolition, grading, and any other applicable work as may be 
required by any governmental authority; (iii) local, county, and state sanitary sewer approvals as may 
be required under applicable law; (iv) local, county, and state water approvals as may be required 
under applicable law; and/or (v) any state or federally required environmental permits, inspections, 
or coordination. This Agreement shall not be construed to grant or endorse any such governmental 
approvals. 

3 
3.
Plans. In addition to Paragraph 2 above, Developer has caused to be prepared, and
will continue to prepare, any and all engineering construction, demolition, or development plans 
and specifications necessary to implement the Developer Work (collectively the “Plan”). Once 
approved by the relevant governmental authorities, the Plan, as it is approved and modified with 
the required approvals, shall thereafter be incorporated into and made a part of this Agreement by 
reference. The Developer shall construct the Developer Work in accordance with the Plan as 
approved and described in this Agreement. This Agreement shall not be construed to grant, 
endorse, or approve the Plan. 
4.
Standard of Work, Maintenance, and Repair. Developer will, in accordance
with Owner, contract for Developer Work, protect, maintain, repair, and/or replace those portions 
of the Property to remain (including but not limited to above and below-ground utilities (private 
or public utility service lines (if remaining), private or public utility transmission mains and 
fixtures, certain fixed property, and any certain plant materials or other items specified to remain). 
Any Developer Work requiring replacement or remediation shall be installed in compliance and 
conformance with the Plan as approved by the City. Developer Work shall be installed in a good 
and workmanlike fashion, in a manner acceptable to the City, and in conformance with any 
applicable local, county, state and federal laws, statutes, ordinances, regulations, and standards. 
5.
Indemnity. The Owner, Developer, its contractors, subcontractors, employees, and
its successors or assigns, agree to indemnify and hold the City, its elected officials, employees, 
professionals, agents, servants, successors and assigns, harmless from and against, any and all 
claims, actions, liability, and expenses in connection with injury or loss of life to person or damage 
to property arising from the Developer’s performance of its obligations under this Agreement; 
provided that such indemnification shall not apply to the negligence or intentional misconduct of 
the City, its agents, servants, contractors, employees or representatives. 
6.
Phasing.  Developer intends to execute the Developer Work as specified herein
over a period of no more that eighteen (18) months, in a continuous and uninterrupted fashion. The 
order of the Developer Work is divided into four (4) distinct sections, each of which is shown on 
Exhibit A and hereinafter referred to as a “Phase.” Subject to the Owner and Developer’s 
obligation to obtain the necessary governmental approvals, the Developer shall have the right to 
determine (i) the location of each Phase; (ii) the boundaries of each Phase; (iii) the order in which 
the various Phases will be developed; and (iv) the time at which the construction and installation 
of the Developer Work in a particular Phase will commence. Developer Work shall proceed in as 
timely a manner as practicable; the eighteen (18) month estimated timeline is not to be construed 
as a deadline, is not obligatory, and is based on the information, estimates, and availabilities to-
date. No penalty whatsoever is to be incurred if Developer Work is not completed within the 
estimated timeline or due to Unavoidable Delays. Unavoidable Delays shall mean delays resulting 
from acts or occurrences outside the reasonable control of the party claiming the delay including 
but not limited to pandemics, storms, floods, fires, explosions, or other casualty losses, unusual 
weather conditions, strikes, boycotts, lockouts, or other labor disputes, litigation commenced by 
third parties, acts of any federal, state, or local governmental unit, COVID-19 driven supply chain 
disruptions and material shortages, or acts beyond the reasonable control of Owner and/or 
Developer. 

4 
7.
Completion. Notwithstanding the foregoing or any other provision herein,
“Completion” of Developer Work includes the removal of all Structures and is furthermore defined 
as full City acceptance and closure of open demolition permits on the Structures as determined by 
the City of Cheyenne Chief Building Official (“CBO”) on a Phase-by-Phase basis. This typically 
includes all necessary remediation, reconstruction, site stabilization, and removal of waste and 
construction materials. Upon Completion of each Phase, Owner and/or Developer shall be returned 
any and all security deposit(s) previously paid on the permit application(s). In the event that the 
all Phases of the Developer Work is not completed within twenty-four (24) months of the date of 
this Agreement, then any portion of the Developer Work not completed by that date may not be 
eligible for reimbursement unless otherwise agreed in writing by all parties hereto. 
8.
Bonding and Acceptance. The Developer may post bonds, performance guaranties
and/or security required for the Developer Work pursuant to all applicable law. Any required 
bonds, performance guaranties and security shall be posted prior to the commencement of 
Developer Work in any Phase. Developer reserves the right to post said instruments on the entirety 
of the project or as appropriate by Phase. Promptly after the completion of the Developer Work as 
bonded, the CBO will perform a final inspection, and subject to the limitations and requirements 
of applicable law and this Agreement, the performance guaranties and security shall be released, 
including any and all security deposit(s) previously paid on the permit application(s) as otherwise 
noted herein. 
9.
Reimbursement.
A. The price or prices stated in the Plan and Developer Work are based on estimated
quantities of materials requiring disposal, fees for said disposal, prices for
component materials, labor rates applicable to the Developer Work, in effect as of
the date of this Agreement are estimates. If, at any time prior to the completion of
performance of the Developer Work to be performed pursuant to the Plan, any of
such material prices, labor rates, freight rates, or other Developer Work rates may
be increased or decreased, based on actual values or quantities; and in respect to
any of the Developer Work performed hereunder there shall be a corresponding
increase or decrease in the prices stated in the Plan, but shall not exceed a total of
Tax Increment allocation of $1.66 million.
B. Developer grants, conveys, and assigns to Lender the right to receive all
reimbursements from the City for the actual and reasonable costs incurred for
Developer Work, reimbursement shall occur from the actual tax increment
allocation generated by the property, as additional security for the loan granted from
Lender to Developer. Lender’s right to receive reimbursement from the City shall
continue until the balance of the loan is paid in full or otherwise agreed to in writing
and signed by Developer, and Lender, and delivered to the City.
C. Upon Completion and Acceptance of Developer Work on each Phase, and when
sufficient Tax Increment has been collected, Lender is entitled to receive
reimbursement in full, from the City, of the actual and reasonable costs incurred for
Developer Work on the completed Phase. Said costs, and the value of
reimbursement, requires written submittal by Developer of verified, third-party
substantiated, record(s) of costs and expenses. Following Completion of each Phase

5 
and Acceptance, Developer shall, within ninety (90) upon completion of each Phase 
of Developer Work, submit a written reimbursement request to City along with all 
said verified record(s) (the “Request”).  
D. City shall have thirty (30) days to respond to Owner’s and/or Developer’s Request
in writing indicating either (i) acceptance in full of the Request; (ii) dispute of the
Request in whole or in part; or (iii) ask for further information to substantiate or
verify the cost(s) in the Request. Any City response other than full acceptance of
the Request shall include a timeline for Developer to respond, being no less than
fifteen (15) days following the date of the City’s response.
E. Following acceptance of the substantiated Request, City shall provide payment of
reimbursement within forty-five (45) days of written acceptance. Notwithstanding
any other provision in this Agreement to the contrary, (i) the City’s obligation to
reimburse Lender pursuant to this Section is limited as defined in the Term Sheet
attached hereto and incorporated herein in its entirety; and (ii) the City’s
reimbursement and payment obligations under the terms of this Agreement are
specifically not a general obligation of the City and are only payable from the net
proceeds (net of the City’s expenses) special fund actually received by the City
generated by the Property, which special fund is comprised of the portion of taxes
payable to the City pursuant to W.S. § 15-9-120 of the URA Code, and Owner,
Developer, and Lender waive any right to claim any payment from the City
pursuant to this Agreement except to the extent of net revenues actually received in
such special fund. Any reimbursement shall not exceed the balance of the special
fund account at the time of request.
F. BY THEIR SIGNATURE AFFIXED HERETO, OWNER, DEVELOPER, AND
LENDER ACKNOWLEDGE AND AGREE THAT NOTWITHSTANDING ANY
OTHER PROVISION IN THIS AGREEMENT, THE OBLIGATIONS OF THE
CITY UNDER THIS AGREEMENT ARE LIMITED SOLELY TO THE NET
PROCEEDS (NET OF THE CITY’S EXPENSES) SPECIAL FUND ACTUALLY
RECEIVED BY THE CITY, WHICH SPECIAL FUND IS COMPRISED OF THE
PORTION OF TAXES PAYABLE TO THE CITY PURSUANT TO W.S. § 15-9-
120 OF THE URA CODE, AND THE OBLIGATIONS OF THE CITY
HEREUNDER ARE NOT A GENERAL OBLIGATION OF THE CITY AND
THE OWNER, DEVELOPER, AND LENDER MAY NOT INSTITUTE A SUIT
AGAINST THE CITY TO RECOVER AMOUNTS DUE TO DEVELOPER,
OWNER OR LENDER FROM ANY OTHER SOURCE OTHER THAN THE
FOREGOING SPECIAL FUND UNDER THE URA CODE.
G. “Soft Costs” are eligible for reimbursement, including, but not limited to,
Administrative Costs, Permit and Application Fees and any other “hidden fee(s)”
which are not substantiated by an invoice or verified expense of funds.
Disposal/dump fees for disposal of Structures demolition waste are eligible for
reimbursement.  Loan interest is also eligible for reimbursement.
H. To the extent that the Developer receives reimbursement from any third party that
is not affiliated with the Owner or Developer or City, then such reimbursement

6 
shall be deemed to reduce the maximum reimbursement available pursuant to this 
Agreement. The obligations of the City to make reimbursements pursuant to this 
Section shall adhere to the Term Sheet attached hereto and incorporated herein in 
its entirety. Notwithstanding the provisions of this Section, the City’s obligation to 
make any reimbursement payment under this Agreement shall be subject to 
satisfaction of the following conditions precedent:  
i. The Owner and Developer shall be in material compliance with all the terms
and provisions of this Agreement at the time of reimbursement; and
ii. The Developer Work shall be completed and fully assessed, and a certificate
shall have been issued for the completion.
I.
The Request for reimbursement shall include all supporting materials and details as
may be required by the City to substantiate the reimbursement and to comply with
the URA Code. Prior to the City having any obligation to make a reimbursement
pursuant to this Section, the City shall have a reasonable opportunity to inspect and
approve any and all Developer Work. Owner and Developer agree that they shall
permit designated representatives of City, upon reasonable notice to Developer, to
enter upon the Property during construction of the Developer Work to inspect such
construction and progress thereof.
10.
Potential Additional Construction Obligations. Due to the nature of Developer
Work under this Agreement, no construction is anticipated or proposed. The parties acknowledge 
that the Developer Work described in the Plan does not provide any infrastructure and that 
development of the Property specifically, and the URA generally may be constrained due to 
limitations imposed by applicable environmental or other laws, statutes, rules, regulations, 
ordinances or other physical conditions or entitlements or lack thereof. 
11.
Recordation. This Agreement shall be recorded in the office of the City Clerk of
the City of Cheyenne; any cost of said recordation or filing shall be at the expense of the Owner. 
12.
Representations and Warranties of Owner. Owner hereby represents and
warrants to the City that it is a Wyoming limited liability company duly organized, in good 
standing, and authorized to do business in the State of Wyoming. Owner is authorized to execute 
and deliver this Agreement to perform its obligations hereunder, which actions do not require the 
consent or approval of any third party, except as otherwise specified herein. 
13.
Representations and Warranties of Developer.  Developer hereby represents and
warrants to the City that it is a Wyoming limited liability company duly organized, in good 
standing, and authorized to do business in the State of Wyoming. Further, Developer is in the 
construction and real estate development business and possesses the requisite experience and 
expertise to complete the Developer Work in accordance with the terms of this Agreement. 
Developer is authorized to execute and deliver this Agreement to perform its obligations 
hereunder, which actions do not require the consent or approval of any third party, except as 
otherwise specified herein. 
14.
Insurance and Waiver of Subrogation. During the term of this Agreement, the
Developer shall maintain comprehensive general liability insurance coverage (which shall include 

7 
worker’s compensation coverage in statutory amounts) in amounts of no less than $3,000,000 per 
incident and $1,000,00 per occurrence. Such coverage shall name the City as an additional insured 
and shall be with companies reasonably acceptable to the City. All policies shall be in a form 
reasonably acceptable to the City. Prior to the commencement of the Developer Work, the 
Developer shall provide the City with a copy of a paid policy or a certificate issued by the insurer 
certifying the existence of the coverage described herein. All insurance policies shall include a

non-renewal. To the extent that any loss or claim results from the performance of any parties’ 
performance of their obligations hereunder and is covered by insurance then the parties waive their 
respective rights of subrogation as against each other. 
15.
Governmental Immunity. The City, along with its officials and employees, does not
waive its governmental immunity by entering into this Agreement, except to the extent necessary 
for the parties to pursue a contract action to clarify or enforce the written terms of the Agreement.  
Furthermore, the City specifically retains all immunities and defenses available to it as a sovereign 
or governmental entity pursuant to W.S. § 1-39-101, et seq., and all other relevant state and federal 
law. Designations of venue, choice of law, enforcement actions, and similar provisions should not 
be constructed as a waiver of governmental immunity. The parties agree that any ambiguity in this

ambiguity as to governmental or sovereign immunity shall be construed in favor of governmental

16.
Relationship of Parties.  Nothing contained in this Agreement shall be deemed
or construed, either by the parties hereto or by any third party, to create the relationship of 
principal and agent or create any partnership, joint venture, or other association between the 
City and the Owner, Developer, or Lender. 
17.
Default.
A. In the event that any party fails to perform its duties and obligations under this
Agreement after ten (10) days written notice (provided that if the failure to perform
is of such nature that it cannot reasonably be cured in ten (10) days, then there shall
be no default so long as the party is making a diligent and good faith effort to correct
the deficiency) then there shall be an event of default and the aggrieved party shall
have any and all rights to which it is entitled at law or in equity, including the right
to seek immediate injunctive relief in a court of competent jurisdiction
notwithstanding the provisions set forth above.
B. Whenever an event of default of this Agreement occurs and is continuing after
proper notice and the period to cure has lapsed, as described above, the City, as
specified below, may take any one or more of the following actions after:
i. The City may suspend its performance under this Agreement until it receives
assurances from the defaulting party or parties, deemed adequate by the City, that
the defaulting party or parties will cure its default and continue its performance
under this Agreement;
ii. The City may terminate this Agreement;

8 
iii. The City may withhold the certificate of completion;
iv. The City shall have no obligation thereafter to make any payments for the
Developer Work;
v. The City shall be entitled to recover from the defaulting party or parties, taking
any action, including legal action, it deems necessary to recover, and Developer
shall repay to the City, an amount equal to the full amount of any payments for
the Developer Work previously made to Lender, with interest thereon at the
highest rate permitted by State law; and
vi. The City may take any action, including legal, equitable, or administrative action,
which may appear necessary or desirable to enforce performance and observance
of any obligation, agreement, or covenant of the Owner and/or Developer, as the
case may be, under this Agreement.
C. In addition to the foregoing in the event any patent or latent defect in the Developer
Work is discovered by City, City shall have the right to offset the cost to cure such
defect against any amount otherwise reimbursable under this Agreement.
D. No remedy herein conferred upon or reserved to the City is intended to be exclusive
of any other available remedy or remedies, but each and every remedy shall be
cumulative and shall be in addition to every other remedy given under this
Agreement or now or hereafter existing at law or in equity or by statute.
E. No delay or omission to exercise any right or power accruing upon any default shall
impair any such right or power or shall be construed to be a waiver thereof, but any
such right and power may be exercised from time to time and as often as may be
deemed expedient.
18.
Replat of Property – Owner intends to replat the Property and may subdivide the
property into one or more tracts or lots to accommodate various commercial, and office uses in 
accordance with Resolution No. 6167, adopted August 23, 2021, as it may be amended. The replat 
may separate the current property into one or more lots or tracts. It being the intent for the 
individual lots or tracts to be sold to appropriate third parties to develop commercial 
improvements.  
19.
Assignment by Owner. Once the Property is replatted, the Owner shall have the
right to assign its rights, duties, and obligations under this Agreement to a single entity that owns 
all or a portion of the Property, as it may be described in a replat, with the City’s and Lender’s 
written consent which shall not be unreasonably withheld, conditioned or denied. The obligations 
of the Owner hereunder shall be binding on all successors and assigns and shall run with the land. 
20.
Assignment by Developer. The Developer shall have the right to assign its rights,
duties, and obligations under this Agreement, in whole or in part, to a single entity with similar 
areas of expertise and experience, with the City’s and Lender’s written consent which shall not be 
unreasonably withheld, conditioned or denied. In the event of such assignment, the succeeding 

9 
developer must post a substitute performance guaranty or security in a form acceptable to the City. 
The obligations of the Developer hereunder shall be binding on all successors and assigns and shall 
run with the land. 
21.
Term. The term of this Agreement shall commence upon its execution and shall
continue until the earlier of (i) the maximum term for completion of the Developer Work as set 
forth above; (ii) the date on which the Lender or in the total aggregate between the Lender and 
Owner, in accordance with Section 9(b), have received reimbursement in the maximum amount 
set forth herein; or (iii) a period of twenty-five (25) years after the date and year this Agreement 
has been executed by all parties or the maximum duration of the TIF until such time the 
Developer Work has been reimbursed, whichever is longer,  at which point this Agreement 
will expire. 
22.
Retroactive Effect. Once fully executed, this Agreement shall be deemed effective
between the Parties from the date the Developer began Development Work on the Property. 
Specifically, the Developer shall be able to submit include costs for work already performed and 
materials already used in Development Work on any reimbursement Request submitted to the City, 
as described in Section 9. 
23.
Governing Law. The terms and conditions of this Agreement shall be governed and
construed in accordance with the laws of the State of Wyoming. 
24.
Counterparts. This Agreement may be executed in multiple counterparts, each
of which shall be deemed an original, but all of which, together, shall constitute one and the 
same instrument. 
25.
Applicable Law.  This Agreement shall be governed by and construed and
enforced in accordance with the laws of the State of Wyoming. 
26.
Entire Agreement.  This Agreement (including the Exhibits attached hereto)
contains the entire agreement between the parties with respect to the subject matter hereof and 
supersedes all prior and contemporaneous representations, statements, understandings, 
negotiations and agreements, oral or written, between the parties, if any, with respect thereto. 
27.
Headings. The article headings contained in this Agreement are for reference only
and for the convenience of the parties. They shall not be deemed to constitute a part of this 
Agreement nor shall they alter, modify or supersede the contents of any of the provisions 
themselves. 
28.
Severability. If any of the provisions of this Agreement are deemed invalid, void or
illegal, then the provisions so affected shall be deleted from this Agreement. All remaining 
provisions shall remain in full force and effect. 
29.
Further Assurances. City, Lender, Developer, and Owner agree that each will take
any and all action including but not limited to the execution of any documentation which may be 
necessary to effectuate the intent of the parties as set forth in this Agreement.
