EXHIBIT B 
HITCHING POST DEMOLITION DEVELOPER WORK AND ESTIMATED COSTS 
I.
SCOPE OF WORK
A. GENERAL: Developer shall furnish and provide all labor, materials, tools, equipment
and machinery, unless otherwise specified, necessary to perform and complete, in a
good and workmanlike manner, the work outlined in this document. The easterly
approximately seventy feet (70’) of the property is not part of the project scope;
all structures, utilities, curbs, sidewalks, etc. in the easterly seventy feet of the
property are to remain as-is.
B. SCOPE OF WORK : Developer shall perform the following in connection with this
contract:
(a)
Abatement of all contaminated and Asbestos Containing Materials (ACM) on the
property as required by WDEQ.
(b)
Demolition of all structures on the property as shown in Attachment A.
(c)
Demolition/removal of all freestanding enclosures and walls, concrete curb and
gutter, sidewalks and patios on the property (adjacent to the buildings)
(d)
Remove all foundation walls to a point at least four feet (4’) below finished
floor elevation.
(e)
Removal of all trash, demolition material and debris.
(f)
Protection of existing trees, public sidewalks, signs, utilities and other items that
are to remain on and adjacent to the site.
a. It is understood that some trees will necessarily be removed. Developer shall
provide a per-unit cost for:
i.
removal of tree less than twenty feet (20’) tall; and
ii.
removal of tree greater than twenty feet (20’) tall.
b.
City of Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities Sanitary Sewer and Domestic Water
Mains and those Black Hills Energy Underground and Overhead Electrical
Lines falling within seventy feet (70’) from the easterly property line (as shown
on “HitchingPost_TOPO SURVEY May2017.pdf”) are to remain.
(g)
Obtain all required permits and payment of all applicable fees (unless otherwise
specified herein).
(h) Verify all utilities service lines, prior to demolition, have been disconnected.
(i)
Developer shall provide project perimeter fencing to delineate project boundaries
and protect inadvertent entry to site. Developer may elect to provide additional
security and/or security fencing as required by City or Developer’s specific needs.
Developer shall be responsible for all costs associated with fencing and/or security
needs.
(j)
Developer shall maintain access to, and from, adjacent properties as required.

2 
C. DEMOLITION
(a)
Demolition shall be conducted in a timely manner.
(b) All demolished materials become the property of the Developer, unless otherwise
indicated herein, or otherwise negotiated, and shall be promptly removed from the
site.
(c)
The Developer shall remove all equipment, machinery, trade or other fixtures remaining
in the building.
(d) All damage incurred in the demolition operation to structures, walks, paving or other
property to remain shall be the responsibility of the Developer; he shall pay all costs
resulting from such damage.
(e)
The demolition shall be conducted in strict accordance with all laws, ordinances
and codes having jurisdiction.
(f)
The Developer shall review reports provided by the Owner in regards to asbestos
and lead-based materials contained within the structures and remove and dispose of
such material in accordance with federal, state, and local laws.
(g) All existing utilities disconnects will be completed to the standards and requirement
of the City of Cheyenne and/or franchised utilities providers, as applicable.
Developer shall verify disconnect prior to start of demolition; with the exception of
service line to remain for project facilitation.
(h)
Developer shall be responsible for the removal and proper disposal of all materials and
shall provide the City with landfill receipts prior to release of payment. Debris to
be delivered to qualified landfill in accordance with state and local codes and
ordinances. All loads shall be covered and secured prior to and during transport.
The Developer relieves the Owner of all liability in the disposal of debris. The
Developer is responsible for all fees and permits, if applicable, except as explicitly
provided for herein.
(i)
During demolition operations, the Developer shall keep the work wetted down to
prevent dust and dirt rising.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-04-25_TotalExtraction_91527988.pdf
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
April 20, 2022 
SUBJECT: 
Contract Modification #1 to Bid E-7-21 for Ice Control Product, for the City of 
Cheyenne Street & Alley Division  
______________________________________________ 
Attached to this Memo is Contract Modification #1 to Bid E-7-21 For Ice Control Product, for the 
City of Cheyenne Street & Alley Division. 
 
This intended purpose of this Contract Modification is to add a retroactive Fuel Escalator, to adjust 
for increasing fuel costs. 
 
This is in response to a request received by the City, from the awarded Bidder, Desert Mountain 
Corp.  This modification proposes an addition of a not-to-exceed amount of $50,000.00 to cover 
fuel escalations for the remainder of the Contract period. 
 
Funding:   
020-26-2616-42-32304 
(1% Sales Tax 19-22 / Salt) $50,000.00 
 

CITY OF CHEYENNE 
CONTRACT MODIFICATION 
 
Contract Modification: #1 
Project:   Ice Control Product, for the City of Cheyenne Street &Alley Division 
 
 
Project Number: 
Bid #E-7-21 
Contractor: 
Desert Mountain Corp, Riverton, WY 
 
Date Prepared:   April 19, 2022 
 
 
 
The above referenced Bid Consideration is amended or modified as set forth in the following and 
these amendments and modifications of the plans and specifications are agreed to by the parties. All 
other aspects of the original agreement remain unchanged and in full force and effect. 
 
I. Description of Changes 
 
Due to increased fuel costs required to transport and deliver Ice Control Product, the Contractor 
(Desert Mountain Corp) has requested a retroactive application of a fuel escalator be applied to all 
Ice Control Product ordered & delivered from January 18, 2022, until the Contract expires.  This 
delivery date (1/18/2022), sets the base fuel rate for the fuel escalator, and the cost is escalated or de-
escalated based on the weekly fuel price published by the U.S. Energy Information Administration 
(EIA), found here: 
https://www.eia.gov/dnav/pet/hist/LeafHandler.ashx?n=PET&s=EMD_EPD2D_PTE_R40_DPG&f=W 
 
The template used for calculation of fuel escalation is based on a template provided and utilized by 
the Wyoming Department of Transportation (WYDOT) for the application of a fuel escalator.   
 
Original Bid Award was for a Two (2) Year Term.  Each year allows for the purchase of 1,000 Tons, 
with an option to purchase an additional 2,000 Tons if needed.  Total cost is $115.00 Per Ton.  For a 
total potential award of $345,000.00 per year.  The City is currently in the second year of the Bid 
award. 
 
II. New Contract Items 
 
Addition and Inclusion of Fuel Escalator, to include retroactive application from January 18, 2022 
through the end of the Contract Term (Fall 2022).   
 
As of the date this Modification is prepared, with the application of the fuel escalator, there is 
$7,132.06 in retroactive fuel escalation costs.  The City of Cheyenne, Public Works Department, 
Street and Alley Division is requesting a not-to-exceed amount of Fifty Thousand Dollars 
($50,000.00) to utilize for the payment of fuel escalation, in accordance with the Contractors request. 
 This includes retroactive application beginning on January 18, 2022, and will continue until the 
contract term ceases, or the fuel escalator funds have been fully utilized. 
 
III. Schedule 
 
No impact expected to schedule or delivery timing of Ice Control Product. 
 
IV. Summary 
Original Contract Amount .................................................................................$690,000.00 
Previous Modifications ......................................................................................................$0 
Contract Amount Prior to this Modification ......................................................$690,000.00 
This Modification ................................................................................................$50,000.00 
New Contract Amount .......................................................................................$740,000.00 
By Stefanie Boster at 10:42 pm, Apr 19, 2022

City of Cheyenne

Mayor Patrick Collins

If fuel adjustments increases beyond the values shown on this chart, the same formula shall apply i.e. for every $0.04 price 
increase an additional .50% change may be added to the price per ton.
Fuel Surcharge Policy
The City of Cheyenne will use the US Department of Energy’s No 2 Diesel Prices to adjust pricing on a weekly basis. The 
base price will be from January 18, 2022,  for the Rocky Mountain Region, which is $3.698.
A fuel adjustment chart (see below) shall be used to determine a fuel adjustment when the price of diesel fuel 
increases/decreases $0.20 cents above/below the base rate. The fuel allowance for shipments on any given day shall be 
based upon that week’s price of diesel fuel as posted on the index above. The weekly adjustment shall be applied based on 
the increase or decrease of the price of diesel fuel versus the base price for the week shown.
The City of Cheyenne will allow a fuel escalation/de-escalation calculation for the week when delivery is required.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-04-25_TotalExtraction_22316065.pdf
Page 1 of 8 
Contract for Public Defense Services 
The City of Cheyenne, referred to as “the City,” and Lance & Hall LLP, referred to 
hereafter as “the Firm,” agree to the provision of public defense services as outlined below for the 
period April 19, 2022 to April 19, 2023 (one year). The City’s Representative is the Mayor, Patrick 
Collins, and the Representative of the Firm is Elizabeth B. Lance. 
Following are the underlying bases for the Contract: 
• 
The City, through the Cheyenne Municipal Court has a constitutionally mandated 
responsibility to provide public defender services in certain criminal cases where 
Defendants are indigent (or otherwise qualify). 
• 
The City desires to have legal services performed for eligible persons entitled to public 
representation in Cheyenne Municipal Court by the Firm, as authorized by law. 
• 
The Firm agrees to provide, and the City agrees to pay for, competent, zealous 
representation to its clients as required by the controlling Wyoming Rules of Professional 
Conduct for Attorneys at Law. 
• 
The City and the Firm agree that any and all funds provided pursuant to this Contract are 
provided for the sole purpose of provision of legal services to eligible clients of the Firm. 
The parties agree as follows: 
I. Duration of Contract 
This Contract shall commence on April 19, 2022 and terminate on December 31, 2023, unless 
extended or terminated earlier in a manner allowed by this Contract. 
II. Definitions 
The following definitions control the interpretation of this Contract: 
A. Eligible client means a defendant (adult or juvenile) who is facing criminal charges in 
the Cheyenne Municipal Court determined by a finding by the sitting Municipal Court 
Judge to be entitled to a court-appointed attorney. 
B. Case; Case Completion: A Case shall mean representation of one defendant in a criminal 
matter (whether by information or citation(s)). Completion of a case is deemed to occur 
when all necessary legal action has been taken during the following period(s): In criminal 
cases, from arraignment through disposition, from arraignment through the necessary 
withdrawal of counsel after the substantial delivery of legal services, or from the entry of 
counsel into the case (where entry into the case occurs after arraignment through no fault 
of the Firm) through disposition or necessary withdrawal after the substantial delivery of 
legal services. Nothing in this definition prevents the Firm from providing necessary legal 
services to an eligible client prior to arraignment, but payment for such services will require 
a showing pursuant to the Extraordinary Expenses paragraph below.  
By Stefanie Boster at 11:52 am, Apr 20, 2022

Page 2 of 8 
C. Disposition: Disposition in criminal cases shall mean: 1) the dismissal of charges, 2) the 
entering of an order of deferred prosecution, 3) an order or result requiring a new trial, 4) 
imposition of sentence, or 5) deferral of any of the above coupled with any other hearing 
on that cause number, including but not limited to misdemeanor probation review, that 
occurs within thirty (30) days of sentence, deferral of sentence, or the entry of an order of 
deferred prosecution. No hearing that occurs after 30 days of any of the above will be 
considered part of case disposition for the purpose of this Contract except that a restitution 
hearing ordered at the time of original disposition, whether it is held within 30 days or 
subsequently, shall be included in case disposition. Disposition includes the filing of a 
notice of appeal, if applicable. Nothing in this definition prevents the Firm from providing 
necessary legal services to an eligible client after disposition, but payment for such services 
will require a showing pursuant to the Extraordinary Expenses paragraph below.  
D. Representational Services: The services for which the City is to pay the Firm are 
representational services, including lawyer services, investigation and appropriate 
sentencing advocacy and social work services, and legal services including but not limited 
to interviews of clients and potential witnesses, legal research, preparation and filing of 
pleadings, negotiations with the appropriate prosecutor and court regarding possible 
dispositions, and preparation for and appearance at all court proceedings. The services for 
which the City is to pay the Firm do not include extraordinary expenses incurred in the 
representation of eligible clients. The allowance of extraordinary expenses at the cost of 
the City will be determined by a court of competent jurisdiction in accordance with relevant 
state statute, court rule, and constitutional provisions. 
E. Misappropriation of Funds: Misappropriation of funds is the appropriation of funds 
received pursuant to this Contract for purposes other than those sanctioned by this Contract. 
The term shall include the disbursement of funds for which prior approval is required but 
is not obtained. 
 
III. Independent Contractor 
The Firm is, for all purposes arising out of this Contract, an independent contractor, and neither 
the Firm nor its employees shall be deemed employees of the City. The Firm shall complete the 
requirements of this Contract according to the Firm's own means and methods of work, which shall 
be in the exclusive charge and control of the Firm and which shall not be subject to control or 
supervision by the City, except as specified herein. 
IV. Firm's Employees and Equipment 
The Firm agrees that it has secured or will secure at the Firm's own expense, all persons, 
employees, and equipment required to perform the services contemplated/required under this 
Contract. 
 

Page 3 of 8 
V. Minimum Qualifications for Firm Attorneys 
A.  Every Firm attorney shall satisfy the minimum requirements for practicing law 
in Wyoming, including active licensure, as determined by the Wyoming Supreme Court. 
Seven hours of each year’s required continuing legal education credits shall be spent in 
courses relating to criminal law practice and/or procedure or other areas of law in which 
the Firm provides legal services to eligible clients under the terms of this Contract. The 
Firm will maintain for inspection on its premises records of compliance with this provision. 
B.  Failure on the part of the Firm to use attorneys and/or staff with the appropriate amount 
of experience or to supervise appropriately its attorneys shall be considered a 
material breach of this Contract.  
VI. Performance Requirements 
The Firm agrees to provide the services and comply with the requirements of this Contract. Any 
material breaches of this agreement on the part of the Firm or the City may result in action as 
described in this Agreement. 
The Firm agrees to provide representational services in the following types of cases: Criminal 
Misdemeanor (TRT, COM, and CRM) 
The Firm agrees to staff its cases according to the following provisions: 
A.  Continuity of representation at all stages of a case, sometimes referred to as 
“vertical” representation, promotes efficiency, thoroughness of representation, and 
positive attorney/client relations. The Firm agrees to make reasonable efforts to 
continue the initial attorney assigned to a client throughout all cases assigned in this 
Contract. Nothing in this section shall prohibit the Firm from making necessary 
staff changes or staff rotations at reasonable intervals, or from assigning a single attorney to 
handle an aspect of legal proceedings for all clients where such method of assignment is in 
the best interest of the eligible clients affected by such method of assignment. 
B. Firm agrees an attorney will contact defendant(s) in assigned cases within seventy-two 
(72) hours from the Cheyenne Municipal Court appointing the Firm to a particular case(s). 
C.  Conflicts of interest may arise in numerous situations in the representation of 
indigent defendants. The Firm agrees to screen all cases for conflict(s) upon assignment 
and throughout the discovery process, and to notify the City promptly if/when a conflict is 
discovered. The Firm will refer to the Wyoming Rules of Professional Conduct for 
Attorneys at Law, as interpreted by Wyoming State Bar Association and/or opinions of the 
Wyoming Supreme Court, and to the American Bar Association Standards for Criminal 
Justice in order to determine the existence and appropriate resolution of conflicts. 
VII. Attorney Training 
Ongoing professional training is a necessity in order for an attorney to keep abreast of changes and 
developments in the law and assure continued rendering of competent assistance of counsel. The 

Page 4 of 8 
Firm shall provide sufficient training, whether in-house or through a qualified provider of CLE, to 
keep all its attorneys who perform work under this Contract abreast of developments in relevant 
law, procedure, and court rules.  
VIII. Compensation and Method of Payment 
A. For the term of this contract, the City shall pay the Firm a rate of $90.00 per billable 
hour of work on assigned cases. Payments will be made on a monthly basis. It is possible 
that the actual amount of compensation will vary according to other terms of this Contract.  
B. The Firm shall provide the City—Mayor’s Office—an itemized billing invoice for all 
hours of work performed on each assigned case by close of business on the last Friday of 
each calendar month. Additionally, for each assigned case, the Firm shall provide a 
breakdown of tasks performed (appearance, research, drafting pleadings, consultation, etc.) 
by tenths of an hour. For example 
Case Number  
Defendant Name 
Task 
Hours/Minutes 
2021-COM-0000 John Doe 
Draft entry of appearance .1 
D. In the event of Firm failure to substantially comply with any items and conditions of 
this Contract or to provide in any manner the work or services as agreed to herein, the City 
reserves the right to withhold any payment until corrective action has been taken or 
completed. This option is in addition to and not in lieu of the City's right to termination as 
provided herein. 
IX. Establishment and Maintenance of Records 
A. The Firm agrees to maintain accounts and records, including personnel, 
property, financial, and programmatic records, which sufficiently and properly reflect all 
direct and indirect costs of services performed in the performance of this 
Contract, including the time spent by the Firm on each case. 
B. The Firm agrees to maintain records which sufficiently and properly reflect all direct 
and indirect costs of any subcontracts or personal service contracts. Such records shall 
include, but not be limited to, documentation of any funds expended by the Firm for said 
personal service contracts or subcontracts, documentation of the nature of the service 
rendered, and records which demonstrate the amount of time spent by each subcontractor 
personal service contractor rendering service pursuant to the subcontract or personal 
service contract. 
C. Records shall be maintained for a period of 5 years after termination of this 
Contract unless the City grants permission to destroy such records. 
X. Hold Harmless and Indemnification 
A. The City assumes no responsibility for the payment of any compensation, wages, 
benefits, or taxes by the Firm to Firm employees or others by reason of the Contract. The 
Firm shall protect, indemnify, and save harmless the City, its representatives, officers, 

Page 5 of 8 
agents, and employees from and against any and all claims, costs, and losses whatsoever, 
occurring or resulting from Firm's failure to pay any compensation, wages, benefits or 
taxes except where such failure is due to the City’s wrongful withholding of funds due 
under this Contract. 
B. The Firm agrees that it is financially responsible and liable for and will repay the City 
for any material breaches of this contract including but not limited to misuse of Contract 
funds due to the negligence or intentional acts of the Firm, its officers, employees, 
representatives, or agents. 
C. Each party to this agreement assumes the risk of any liability arising from its own 
conduct. Each party agrees that it will be responsible for any loss, claim, damages or 
demands resulting from its negligence and the negligence of its employees and agents.  
Neither party agrees to insure, defend, or indemnify the other.  The City does not waive 
defenses available to it under the governmental claims act. 
XI. Insurance 
Without limiting the Firm's indemnification, it is agreed the Firm shall maintain in force, at all 
times during the performance of this Contract, a policy or policies of insurance covering its 
operation as described below. 
A. General Liability Insurance 
The Firm shall maintain continuously public liability insurance with limits of liability not 
less than: $250,000 for each person, personal injury, $500,000 for each 
occurrence, property damage, liability, or a combined single limit of $500,000 for each 
occurrence, personal injury and/or property damage liability. 
Such insurance shall include the City as an additional insured and shall not be reduced or 
canceled without 30 days' prior written notice to the City. The Firm shall provide a 
certificate of insurance or, upon written request of the City, a duplicate of the policy as 
evidence of insurance protection. 
B. Professional Liability Insurance 
The Firm shall maintain or ensure that its professional employees maintain professional 
liability insurance for all acts which occur during their employment with the Firm which 
constitute professional services in the performance of this Contract. Specifically, the Firm 
or its professional employees must maintain professional liability insurance in relation to 
representation in appointed cases pursuant to this Agreement. For purposes of this 
Contract, professional services shall mean any services provided by a licensed 
professional. 
Such professional liability insurance shall be maintained in an amount not less 
than $1,000,000 combined single limit per claim/aggregate. The Firm further agrees that 
it shall have sole and full responsibility for the payment of any funds where such payments 
are occasioned solely by the professional negligence of its professional employees and 

Page 6 of 8 
where such payments are not covered by any professional liability insurance, including but 
not limited to the amount of the deductible under the insurance policy.  
Such insurance shall not be reduced or canceled without 30 days' prior written notice to 
the City. The Firm shall provide certificates of insurance or, upon written request of the 
City, duplicates of the policies as evidence of insurance protection. 
XII. Termination and Suspension 
A. The City may terminate this Contract in whole or in part upon 10 days' written notice to 
the Firm in the event that – 
1. The Firm substantially breaches any duty, obligation, or service 
required pursuant to this Contract; 
2. The Firm engages in misappropriation of funds; or 
3. The duties, obligations, or services herein become illegal, or not feasible. 
B. The Firm reserves the right to terminate this Contract without cause upon sixty (60) days 
written notice to the City. 
C. In the event of the termination or suspension of this Contract, the Firm shall continue to 
represent clients that were previously assigned, and the City will be liable for any payments 
owed for the completion of that work. The Firm will remit to the City any monies paid for 
cases not yet assigned or work not performed under the Contract. The City may request 
that the Firm attempt to withdraw from any case assigned and not completed. Should a 
court require, after the Firm has attempted to withdraw, the appearance of counsel from the 
Firm on behalf of any client previously represented by the Firm where such representation 
is no longer the obligation of the Firm pursuant to the terms of this Contract, the City will 
honor payment to the Firm upon judicial verification that continued representation is 
required. 
D. If termination is due to misappropriation of funds, non-performance of the scope of 
services, or fiscal mismanagement, the Firm shall return to the City those funds, 
unexpended or misappropriated, which, at the time of termination, have been paid to the 
Firm by the City. 
E. Otherwise, this Contract shall terminate on the date specified herein, and shall be subject 
to extension only by mutual agreement of both parties hereto in writing. 
XIII. Assignment/Subcontracting 
A. The Firm shall not assign or subcontract any portion of this Contract without consent of 
the City. Any consent sought must be requested by the Firm in writing not less than five 
(5) days prior to the date of any proposed assignment or sub-contract, provided that this 
provision shall not apply to short-term personal service contracts with individuals to 
perform work under the direct supervision and control of the Firm. Short-term personal 
service contracts include any contract for a time less than one year. Any individuals 

Page 7 of 8 
entering such contracts shall meet all experience requirements imposed by this Contract. 
The City shall be notified of any short-term contracts which are renewed, extended, or 
repeated at any time throughout the Contract. 
B. The term "Subcontract" as used above shall not be read to include the purchase 
of support services that do not directly relate to the delivery of legal services under 
the Contract to clients of the Firm. 
C. The term "Personal Service Contract" as used above shall mean a contract for 
the provision of professional services which includes but is not limited to 
counseling services, consulting services, social work services, investigator services and 
legal services. 
XIV. Notices 
Whenever this Contract provides for notice to be provided by one party to another, such notice 
shall be: 
1. In writing; and 
2. Directed to the Chief Executive Officer of the Firm and the director/manager of the City 
department/division specified on page 1 of this Contract. 
Any time limit by which a party must take some action shall be computed from the date that 
notice is received by said party. 
XV. The Parties' Entire Contract/Waiver of Default 
The parties agree this Contract is the complete expression of the terms hereto and any oral 
representations of understanding not incorporated herein are excluded. Both parties recognize that 
time is of the essence in the performance of the provisions of this Contract. Waiver of any default 
shall not be deemed to be a waiver of any subsequent default. Waiver of a breach of any provision 
of this Contract shall not be deemed to be a waiver of any other subsequent breach and shall not 
be construed to be a modification of the terms of this agreement unless stated to be such through 
written mutual agreement of the parties, which shall be attached to the original Contract. 
XVI. Nondiscrimination 
During the performance of this Contract, the Firm or any party subcontracting with the Firm under 
the authority of this Contract shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Wyoming Fair 
Employment Practices Act (Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq.), the Americans With Disabilities Act 
(ADA), as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the Age Discrimination Act of 1975 and any 
properly promulgated rules and regulations thereto, and shall not discriminate against any 
individual on the grounds of age, sex, creed, color, race, religion, national origin, ancestry, 
pregnancy or qualifying disability in connection with the performance under this Agreement. 
The Firm shall comply fully with all applicable federal, state, and local laws, ordinances, executive 
orders, and regulations which prohibit such discrimination. 
 

Page 8 of 8

___________________________________________________

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-04-25_TotalExtraction_22573080.pdf
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
April 20, 2022 
SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #E-18-22 for furnishing Twenty-Nine (29) Trucks, for the 
City of Cheyenne, Community Recreation & Events Department, Fleet 
Maintenance Division, and Animal Control Division 
______________________________________________ 
Bid #E-18-22 was solicited for furnishing Twenty-Nine (29) Trucks, for the City of Cheyenne, 
Community Recreation & Events Department, Fleet Maintenance Division, and Animal Control 
Division. 
A Mandatory Pre-Bid Meeting was held, and Three (3) Bidders (Ken Garff, Greiner Ford, Cowboy 
Chrysler Dodge Jeep Ram), and One (1) Upfitter (Able Equipment) was in attendance.  Two (2) 
bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.   
 
The Bids were received from: 
1. Ken Garff Cheyenne, Cheyenne, Wyoming.  
a. Submitted most responsive bid, not overall lowest cost bid. 
i. Page 34, Item 14, Yes, but struck 3.73, and wrote 4.30 limited slip 
ii. Page 52, Item 14, Yes, but struck 3.73, and wrote 4.30 limited slip 
 
2. Greiner Ford.  Casper, Wyoming.  Bid Rejected, Multiple Bid Exceptions. 
a. Submitted overall lowest cost bid, however bid was least responsive and contained 
multiple Bid Exceptions. 
i. Conditioned bid to request assistance on prepayment on body equipment, 
and potential for cost increases.   
 
“If bids are awarded, we would like assistance on prepaying for equipment 
to hold the price.  We will be out over 300 days waiting for vehicles and 
equipment.  If you decide to wait to order equipment, there would be 
additional manufacturer imposed surcharges passed on.” 
 
ii. Dump Body: Rugby Eliminator was not an approved equal, but Duraclass 
Yardbird was.   
iii. Wyoming Residency Not Selected, No Certificate Provided. 
iv. Page 26, Item 14, Yes, but wrote Limited Slip Axle. 
v. Page 44, Item 16, No. Limited Slip Only Available in 4.30 Ratio. 
vi. Page 45, Item 31, No.  No Radio Installation. 
vii. Page 48, Item 29, No.  No Radio Supply OR Installation. 

The bids were reviewed by Bryan Bell (Fleet Maintenance), Jason Sanchez (CRE), Teresa Moore 
(CRE), Sam Payne (Parks), Mark Ellison (Forestry), Ron Conard (Golf), Tina Worthman 
(Botanic), Mike Wilkinson (Compliance), Eric Fountain (Compliance), and Vicki Nemecek 
(Public Works).   
 
It was determined by the group, and reviewed with the City Attorney’s Office, that of the two (2) 
bids submitted, the Bid from Greiner Ford has been rejected due to multiple Bid exceptions, and 
the Bid Submitted by Ken Garff Cheyenne is the most responsive Bid submission to this Bid 
Request. 
 
The requested Bid award is for a grand total of $1,624,663.00.  This total is for the purchase of 
Twenty-Nine (29) Trucks, and Acceptance of Trade-In Offers on Twenty-Six (26) of the Trade-
Ins.  The City will not accept the Trade-In Offer on Two (2) of the Trades shown on the Bid 
Tabulation, and will hold those units for surplus sale at auction.

______________________________________________ 
Bid #E-24-22 was solicited for the Surplus Sale of City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department 
Evidence Seized Firearms.

recommendation is to accept the highest responsive bid from Proforce Law Enforcement, for the 
Surplus Sale of City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department Owned Firearms.  ProForce Law 
Enforcement did submit a copy of their active Federal Firearms Licenses (FFL) with their Bid. 
 
The total amount of revenue to the City, is $13,198.70.

CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING 
AGREEMENT 
 
Bid No. S-15-22 
 
Contract No. _______ 
 
THIS AGREEMENT, entered into this ____ day of ___________, _____, by and between 
the CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, hereinafter referred to as the “CITY”, and GREAT 
WESTERN INSTALLATIONS, hereinafter referred to as the “CONTRACTOR”. 
 
WITNESSETH that the Contractor and the City, for the considerations stated herein, 
mutually agree as follows: 
 
ARTICLE 1.  STATEMENT OF WORK.  The Contractor shall furnish all supervision, 
technical personnel, labor, materials, machinery, tools, equipment, and services, including utility 
and transportation services, and perform and complete all work in an efficient and workmanlike 
manner in the construction of the “Synthetic Turf Safety Surfacing for Romero Park and Sun 
Valley Park Playgrounds” project all in strict accordance with the Contract Documents including 
all addenda thereto, numbered and dated: Addendum One (1), dated March 31, 2022. 
 
ARTICLE 2.  RESPONSIBLE DESIGNEE FOR THE CITY.  The Contractor shall in any 
and all matters relating to the scope of services to be provided under this Contract or any other 
provisions herein, contact the City Engineer, or his/her designated representative. 
 
ARTICLE 3.  TIME FRAME FOR COMPLETION.  The services to be performed under 
this Agreement shall commence on the date stipulated in the “Notice to Proceed” that will be issued 
by the City.  The work shall be completed by October 30, 2022.  If the work has not been 
completed within the time stipulated above, including any extensions of time issued by the City 
for excusable delays, the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City fixed, agreed liquidated 
damages, as stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar day of delay until the 
work is completed. 
 
ARTICLE 4.  COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT.  The CITY will pay 
the CONTRACTOR for the performance of the Contract in current funds, the sum of One 
Hundred Forty-Three Thousand, Fifty-One Dollars, and Eighty-Eight Cents ($143,051.88).  
In the event there are changes in the estimated quantities shown on the Bid Proposal, the unit prices 
multiplied by the actual quantities shall govern, and the total contract amount will be adjusted 
accordingly.  The City agrees to pay the above amount for contractual services in the following 
manner, upon receipt of appropriate documentation: 
 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for percentage of estimated work completed.  
Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the payable due date as established 
annually by the City Treasurer’s Office.  The pay request shall be submitted on the Contract 
Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or the AIA Documents G702 and G703.  
The engineer will review the estimate for approval prior to payment. 
 
 
By Stefanie Boster at 10:49 pm, Apr 19, 2022

b. 
The City will withhold five percent (5%) of the dollar value of the work completed for a 
minimum of forty-one (41) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement has been published 
in accordance with Wyo. Stat. §16-6-116.  Upon completion of the work under this Contract, 
the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of Completion; the Consent of Surety; 
Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and all Sub-Contractors, Suppliers and 
Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; Affidavit of Payment; and a current Workers 
Compensation Certificate.  Final payment will not be made until the above documents have 
been received by the City and all items on the Punch List have been completed, and the 
advertising requirements have been met. 
 
ARTICLE 5.  CONTRACT.  The executed Contract Documents shall consist of the 
following: 
 
1. This Agreement;  
2. Addenda; 
3. Invitation for Bids; 
4. Instructions to Bidders; 
5. Signed Bid Proposal; 
6. General Conditions and Insurance; 
7. Supplemental Conditions; 
8. Part IV Forms & Notices; 
9. Specifications and Special 
Provisions;  
10. Drawings; and, 
11. Land and Water Conservation Fund 
(LWCF), Project #56-00937,  
Certification Forms
 
This Agreement, together with other documents enumerated in this Article 5, which said other 
documents are as fully a part of the Contract as if hereto attached or herein repeated, forms the 
Contract between the parties hereto. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, THAT the governing body of the City of Cheyenne has authorized 
the Mayor as Executive Officer of the City to enter into this Agreement, and that the parties 
hereto have caused this Agreement to be executed on the day and year in the first part herein 
written.
